# What a finished driver needs (2026-10-10)

The roadmap from today's state to a finished driver: what exists, what is
missing, what can be written now without the card, and what only a card
run can settle. `docs/PLAN-NEXT.md` stays the log of the items already
done; this file decides the order from here.

## Two finish lines

| | **D: desktop driver** | **M: Metal driver** |
|---|---|---|
| What the user gets | macOS on the RX 7800 XT as the only GPU: both monitors, every resolution the monitor offers, hotplug, sleep/wake, idle power near Linux (~12-15 W), stable for days | D + Metal apps run on the GPU (Safari, Preview, Final Cut, games through Metal/MoltenVK), WindowServer composites on the GPU |
| Left (estimate) | ~30-40 % | ~70-80 % |
| Biggest risk | DCN mode setting without the full amdgpu DC/DML | the Metal plugin (NullMoth port) has never run here; how much of Metal it covers decides everything |

Horizon: macOS 26 is the last x86_64 release; the work only pays while it
is supported.

## State per area (updated 2026-10-14, card run 56)

Legend: **card** = verified on the card (`docs/status/hw-verified.txt`),
**sim** = simulator/host tests only, **-** = missing. The amdgpu functions
that apply to this card are all ported (`docs/status/STATUS.md`: 1483 of
1483); what the table lists as sim waits for its card step.

| Area | card | sim only | missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bring-up (PSP, SMU, GMC/GART, IH+MSI, SDMA, GFX, KIQ on MES, KCQ, MES scheduler pipe) | all | kernel queues mapped by the MES scheduler (`rdna3amdgpu=1`), user queues, amdgpu's exact sequence | bare metal cold boot (only the VM so far) |
| Recovery | GFX and DMA hang recovery (`hanggfx`, `8h`) | mode-1 reset, VM faults, BACO | - |
| Power | GFX CG, GFXOFF allowed, UCLK DPM free, idle GFXCLK cap (14-15 W) | non-GFX clock gating, S3 suspend/resume with VRAM eviction, PM control (fan, power, OD, profiles) | idle power down to Linux's ~12 W (the PMFW counts GFX busy) |
| Display | GOP takeover, cursor, EDID names, extended desktop, own mode set with the DISPCLK raise, scaled modes, display sleep, hotplug | DP SST / MST / DSC (also over MST) / UHBR / ODM, HDMI 2.0 with scrambling, audio, output format, gamma, the display state across a reset and a sleep | the DP / DSC / MST / UHBR / 4K HDMI steps need such monitors on the bench |
| UAPI (amdgpu ioctls) | CS on DMA/GFX/compute, fences, BO mmap, USERPTR, PRIME | user queues, timeline syncobj fds; IOSurface BOs (`RD_NR_XNU_SURFACE_BO`, A3, host-tested, not on the card yet) | |
| Vulkan (RADV) | `rdna3_vktest` 13/13: compute, transfer, a triangle through a render pass, image copies, a timeline semaphore | the CTS subset in the report (V2, step `cts`) | WSI / present (V3) |
| Accelerator / WindowServer | desktop composited through it on both heads, flipped zero-copy; copy forward (`submitCopyForward`, from the buffer the head showed last; a no-op when that is the target: `common/rdna3_swap_front.h`, card run 58b's flicker with two backs) | swap copies by SDMA (A2, `rdna3accelsdma=1`) | gates on by default (A1), IOSurface <-> BO (A3: WindowServer composites into per-process copies, 8-10 ms per 1080p frame) |
| Metal | `mtlprobe` compute + render, a CAMetalLayer window on the screen (M4), WindowServer composites on the plugin (M5), Chess and Maps run on it (M6) | - | GPU access to the apps' read-only backing stores (GEM_USERPTR read-only, run 57), point/line draws (mesa 0009), Safari's GPU process (sandbox blocks `/Library/GPUBundles`) |
| Video (VCN 4.0) | - | VCN 4.0 + JPEG 4.0 rings, VIDEO_CAPS and CS, DPG power gating, suspend/resume | the card run (steps `vcn`, `vcn2`, `vcnsleep`); decode/encode from Mesa; VideoToolbox (optional) |
| Packaging | OpenCore in the VM | `package/oc_install.sh`, the installer `.pkg` and tarball from CI | bare metal run of the guide, Tahoe (26) layout on a 26 VM (R4) |

## Work items

Size: S < 300 lines, M 300-1500, L 1500-5000, XL > 5000 (or unknown).
**now** = can be written and verified on the host/simulator today;
**1 boot** = written now, one card run confirms (batched into the round's
single big step); **iterate** = depends on what the card / macOS does,
needs several rounds, cannot be written far ahead.

### A - accelerator / WindowServer (D, prerequisite of M)

| # | Item | Size | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Two heads confirmed by the scanout pictures, then `rdna3accel=1 rdna3accelfb=4 rdna3acceliop=1` default (boot-loop guard kept: `-rdna3off`) | S | 1 boot |
| A2 | Swap copies by SDMA/GPU instead of the CPU (`submitSwapCopy` -> SDMA linear copy), BAR reads avoided | M | now -> 1 boot |
| A3 | IOSurface <-> GEM BO (same VRAM allocation visible to WindowServer and to RADV/Metal): `RD_NR_XNU_SURFACE_BO` gives a BO over the accelerator's allocation for a surface plane (looked up in the caller's task, referenced while the BO lives); libdrm_xnu's `drm_xnu_surface_fd` makes it a dma-buf fd for RADV's OPAQUE_FD import, the Metal plugin uses it where NVK used its RM hook. Card check: plugin log "item 7: ... live in the surface's family VRAM", kernel log "GEM: IOSurface ... (A3)". Open question for the card: if the accelerator ever re-backs a surface (deallocPhysical, then a new allocation) while a BO holds the old range, RADV would draw where WindowServer no longer reads - stale content, not corruption. Security (ECC review): isolation rests on IOSurfaceRoot's lookup in the caller's task, checked by `rdna3_drminfo --surface-xproc` in every report (SURFXPROC: another process's surface must give ENOENT); surface BOs cannot be exported, one file pins at most half the visible VRAM; still open: DIRTY is not tied to a surface the file imported (any process may mark a surface it can look up). Card run 58 (2026-10-15c, first card run): every process shared surfaces, but WindowServer's display framebuffers (the 1920x1080 compositing surfaces) got BOs over a second VRAM copy that no swap scans out (the legacy surface's buffers are other allocations filled by the family's page-on): the main screen kept an old frame. They are kept out of A3 now (`common/rdna3_a3_display.h`, `rdna3a3display=1` lets them in, N27) | L | card run 58: works for app surfaces; display surfaces kept out (N27) |

| N24 | VMIDs held for a process's whole life: one of the 15 hardware VMIDs (7 with `rdna3userq`) per open DRM file, so with the Metal plugin serving every app (N1) the card runs out after about a dozen programs and the next one gets no GPU. amdgpu binds a VMID only while a job runs and takes it back when the job's fence signals (`amdgpu_vmid_grab`, `amdgpu_vmid_grab_idle`, `amdgpu_vmid_grab_reserved`, the VMID manager `amdgpu_vmid_mgr_init`, the page-table flush on a reused VMID `amdgpu_vm_flush`); port that so VMIDs are shared in time. Until then the open is refused cleanly when none is free, at most 8 per uid, the last 2 for root and _windowserver (N18); residuals (ECC security review): one app of the console user can take that uid's share and lock the user's other GPU apps out; after a fast user switch the previous console user keeps its VMIDs and the new one may get none | kext, hw/gpuvm | L | open |
| N25 | GPU hang recovery is triggered by whoever waits 10 s on a fence (`drmFenceWait` -> `recoverHang`), not by how long the job itself has run: a process can still make the kernel reset another process's long job by submitting a NOP behind it and waiting. Port amdgpu's job timeout (`drm_sched` job timeout per ring, `amdgpu_job_timedout`), so recovery follows the running job's own runtime | kext, uapi | M | open |
| N26 | Card run 57: the HDMI boot head's monitor said "no signal" while its OTG ran with the desktop (DP clone fine); driver log identical to run 56, where both worked. Diagnostic build: the TX watch (`hw/dcn32_txstate.c`, `RDNA3,Head<n>,TX`, the looks' `TX` lines). Next run: the `TX at the start` / `after the clone` / `TX change` lines and the look's `TX problems`; when the monitor went dark (from the boot screen, at WindowServer, after a test step). Latent, `rdna3vrr=1` only: `optc32_set_drr` without bounds selects V_TOTAL_MIN / MAX, which on a head the driver did not mode-set may be 0 (the TX watch names it `DRR bound 0`; `displayVrrCommit` runs only after a mode set, which programs them) | hw/dcn32_txstate, RDNA3Resume | M | open (waits for the card) |
| N27 | Card run 58 (2026-10-15c): the main screen kept an old frame (wallpaper and menu bar) while WindowServer's windows flashed in and out; root cause: A3 BOs over WindowServer's display framebuffers, a VRAM copy no swap scans out (flips only ever went to the legacy surface's buffers 0xb100000 / 0xc0e0000 / 0xc8d0000, the BOs sat at 0xd0f0000..0xe8d0000). Interim (done): those surfaces stay out of A3, composited through copies (`rdna3a3display=1` / `debug.rdna3accel_a3display=1` restores the run 58 behaviour; off while `debug.rdna3accel_iop_flip=1`); a display surface is a WindowServer surface of the size a framebuffer scans out at the import (asked live) or of the head's own swaps; the kernel knows the importing process only, not the creator, so WindowServer's import of an application's full-screen surface is refused too and read through copies (not counted apart: IOSurface exposes no creator to the kernel); `a3 display:` lines, `debug.rdna3accel_swaps_h<N>` / `swaps_a3_h<N>`, triage `A3 display` and `M6 screen` (a pipe frozen through the M6 looks). Open: (1) zero-copy for the display: map a legacy swap buffer to its IOSurface and flip the IOSurface's own VRAM when the dirty mark says it is newest, then let display surfaces back in; (2) VRAM is not cleared on allocation (the new WindowServer's buffer at 0xbc50000 showed the old WindowServer's last frame); (3) Terminal (apps mode) TCP reads at an unmapped VA 0x80018223f000 during and after the 960x540 mode switch, 9 times, still after its VM was marked guilty, each followed by 4 VMID 0 CPC writes at address 0 (the 45 VMFaults); WindowServer's GFX job hung at the same mode switch (soft reset, WindowServer restarted) ; card run 58b (2026-10-15d): with the display surfaces out, windows and menus flickered on both screens - WindowServer's 615 other inputs were A3 BOs it also wrote (747 surface-in uploads into shared VRAM) while their owners drew there; every WindowServer import is now kept out by default (`rdna3a3ws=1` / `rdna3a3=0`), A3 stays for applications' own and shared surfaces; (4) open: an ordering of owners' GPU / CPU writes with WindowServer's reads (dirty marks the reader honours, no reader uploads into shared VRAM) before WindowServer's inputs can join A3; the kernel's A B A B flicker counters say whether the copy path flickers too; (5) open: flicker confined to one band of one head (a small window or one status item) reads like a blinking caret and is only a WARN (`swaps_ababwide_h<N>` counts the swaps where more than one band alternated); the 8 bands x 48 columns sample can miss a small item altogether; card run 58b's second run (2026-10-16): the flicker stayed with every WindowServer input kept out - its cause was copy forward copying nothing while two back buffers took turns (fixed, `common/rdna3_swap_front.h`), so whether WindowServer's inputs can go back into A3 is open again once the flicker is gone; (6) open after the ECC review of 60526e4: a copy forward that missed rects keeps them pending for that buffer (8 rects, then the whole picture) and copies them with the next copy forward into it from a source that did not miss them itself (after 3 calls blocked by a source that missed them too, copied anyway: both backs then hold the same stale region until WindowServer redraws it), not reported to CoreDisplay (which would switch copy forward off for good, card run 54) - if the card shows rects stale for long, reporting after a run of misses is the alternative; the record copy forward reads is the buffer the head's last swap presented even when the flip failed (WindowServer's frames, not the panel's); the IOSurface IOP flips (`rdna3accel_iop_flip=1`) and the legacy swaps share the per-head record, a legacy copy forward then finds a foreign buffer and copies nothing | accel, plugin, kext | M | interim done, open (1)-(4) |
| N28 | Card run 2026-10-17 (~10th run of "windows and menus flicker on both screens": a window shows, then the wallpaper where it was): copy forward works now (h1 3080 from the shown buffer, 0 missed) and the A B A B test sees little (1 % / 15 %), but it hashes at swap time only. Code finding: `rd3AccelFlipTo` -> `flipToLocked` programs the HUBP and returns, the flip latches at the next vblank, so `submitSwapCopy` gave WindowServer the old buffer back while the panel still read it (H1). Diagnostic build + fix (`common/rdna3_flip_latch.h`, `tests/test_flip_latch.c`): the swap waits for the latch (`debug.rdna3accel_swap_latch` / boot-arg `rdna3swaplatch`, default 1; amdgpu: the flip event from `dm_pflip_high_irq`), the counters `swaps_latchpend/_latchwait/_latchus/_latchmax/_latchtimeout/_latchunknown/_latchoff_h<N>`, `cf_onpanel_h<N>`, the torn test (`swaps_tornchk/_torn/_inplace/_tornskip_h<N>`, `debug.rdna3accel_swap_torn`), `swaps_shownfreed_h<N>`, `swaps_emptyfilled/_emptystill_h<N>` (triage `flip latch`). H2 (the flip before the GPU finished the frame) has no mechanism in the default configuration: the legacy buffers are written only by the family's page-on (CPU) and copy forward (SDMA, waited for), WindowServer's GPU composite reaches them through the plugin's surface-out after `vkQueueSubmit` was waited for; no GPU-wait sysctl was added (`swaps_a3_h<N>` counts flips of GPU-written A3 BOs, 0 with `rdna3a3display=0`). Open: (1) with one back buffer the zero-copy flip shows the buffer WindowServer draws into (`swaps_inplace_h<N>`): a copy into the front (what `submitSwapCopy` asks for) or a third buffer if the card shows it; (2) the latch wait sleeps in the swap under the family's lock (the family hands the old buffer back when `submitSwapCopy` returns), up to two OTG frames or the cap (2.5 frames, 35-100 ms) per swap, and two heads may take turns there and halve the frame rate (A/B with `swap_latch=0`, the card run measures it); the alternative is completing the swap's `IOAccelEvent` (submitSwapCopy's a0) from the flip-done instead of waiting (a later redesign); a swap waits once, after its own flip, measured from that flip (2.5 of the head's frames from its measured refresh, 35-100 ms; the next swap's entry only looks: review round 2 removed the entry wait, which a shared budget made unable to time out a stuck flip), 3 timeouts in a row turn a head's waits off until a flip is seen latched (logged both ways, `swaps_latchwaitsoff_h<N>`); with `swap_latch=0` the feature sleeps nowhere (the empty-back re-read too); ECC review round 1 fixed the backoff that never recovered, the entry wait ignoring `swap_latch=0`, the 60 Hz-only cap, the silent status, hash and flip failures and triage for archive-only reports; (3) a buffer freed while scanned (`swaps_shownfreed_h<N>`) is only counted; (4) the 320 of 1030 skipped swaps on head 0 (empty back): `swaps_emptyfilled_h<N>` says whether the frame lands late; (5) WindowServer crashed after the mode switch (plugin fence wait 5000 ms, GPU hang) and Terminal's VMID 5 TCP read faults stay open (N27 (3)); (6) the IOP flips (`rdna3accel_iop_flip=1`) do not update the latch record | accel, triage | M | diagnostic + fix, card check open |
| N29 | Card run 2026-10-17b (both screens mostly black, content now and then, everything flickers; the flip latch from N28 works: 0 pending, 0 timeouts): head 0's swaps name the framebuffer resource as the front and two backs in turns; the second back read empty at every swap and the family's aperture access (page-on) never named it; 85 of 229 shown frames rewritten in place; 14 + 10 buffers freed while scanned; at the mode switch the core refused VRAM (0xe00002db, the CPU-visible heap) and CoreDisplay switched copy forward off (0xe00002bc: the family's, the driver answers only success). Root model mismatch (H-COPY): `submitSwapCopy(event, front, back)` is a copy into the front, the back WindowServer's again on return; the driver flipped to the back. The legacy swap's own IOAccelEvent (submitSwapCopy's a0) is NOT completed asynchronously: the family completes it through its stamps (`RD3EventMachine::writeStamp` writes the stamp page at once) and nothing reverse-engineered so far says which stamp a swap's event waits on or what `IOAccelLegacySurface::isBackBufferReady` (traced: 5538 calls in 18b, always 1) does with a 'not ready'; the closest safe form is in place: the buffer WindowServer gets back is the one leaving the panel, and copy forward's target and the family's page-on wait for the latch only when they target it. ECC review round 1 (cpp-reviewer, security-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), all fixed with host tests: (A) the same-buffer skip was decided before the queue's latch look, so a SKIP left an older queued present that the worker then programmed over the newer frame - now `rd3_fq_present` decides inside the queue after the look and only with nothing in flight or queued (test: the stale-frame replay, mutation-checked); (B) a head whose flips stay pending (blanked, DPMS off, stuck HUBP, another path's flip) looped Busy -> given up -> Busy forever with no frame and an IOP transaction never complete - `rd3_fq_verdict` counts such flips and after `RD3_FQ_STUCK_RUN` (3) programs the queued present without NOWAIT (the framebuffer's 20 ms wait then flip anyway; amdgpu's flip_done timeout), `swaps_pq_foreigntimeout_h<N>` / `_giveup_h<N>`, PRESENT 12/13, log `its flips stay PENDING`; reuse-gate waiters are woken at a mode change, a buffer freed, a flip home and stop; a latch wait answered at once (framebuffer stopping, no lock) no longer busy-spins the worker (`_fbwaitfast_h<N>`, `_fbwaitto_h<N>`); (C) the IOP copy fallback's flip home now runs under the queue's lock, drops the queued flip (`rd3_fq_moved`, its pipe signalled, `_home_h<N>`, PRESENT 14) and forgets the latch record; (D) a freed buffer is dropped from the queue and the latch record under one hold of the lock (the worker could program it in between), an in-flight one counted (`_freedinflight_h<N>`); (E) replaced and joined IOP transactions signal their pipe (`_iopdrop_h<N>`); (F) every `gIopFlipFront/Prev` update is under `gPresLock` (`rd3AccelIopFrontMove`: runtime `iop_queue` / `iop_async` toggles double-released); (G) the plugin-wrote mark is a per-allocation generation; (H) the perfstats tick re-arms under a lock and stop waits, cancels and frees it (and the queue's workers: `rd3AccelPresentStop`), start after stop works; (I) triage: counters missing on a current build, a queue that never engaged, `present_init_failed`, `swap_async 0` the boot-args did not ask for, Busy heavy / Busy with no latch (stuck head), `perf_writes` missing, `perf_dictfail` / `_setfail`, `swaps_pq_donemax`, the serial log's `present queue:` lines, INFO that latchpend / latchwait do not measure flips under `swap_async 1`; every new counter checked in the collectors' grep, `A3_SYSCTL` and `PQ_KEYS` by `tests/test_trace_parity.py`. The card must show: smooth movement in mode 0, `swaps_pq_early` / `_dropped` 0, `swaps_pq_wait` small, `swaps_pq_giveup` / `_foreigntimeout` 0 on a lit head (non-zero only while a head is blanked), `swaps_pq_busy` small against `_prog` + `_next`, `present_init_failed` 0, GPU % in Activity Monitor (AFTER-TESTS "DRIVER-PLAN N38"). Then the A/B against the present mode (`present: page flip mode`, `transactions`): `rdna3agdcvendor=1`, `rdna3fbintegrated=1`, `rdna3dispflags=1`, `rdna3glorder=0`, each alone, with `play-desktop` / `play-macpro` / `play-rootport` (TESTING-VM "Driver-side A/B"). Open: (a) closed by N42 (the DCN vblank / pflip interrupts ported, the poll kept as the fallback); the rest need the card or a decision: (b) whether WindowServer's open order was the only cause of the crossed GL indices (the triage row says); (c) GPUWrangler's IG flag cannot be changed from a kext (only `play-rootport`); (d) fb switches are boot-args only (read at start; the fb kext has no sysctls), the AGDC vendor is a runtime sysctl; (e) `rdna3fbdtmap=1` adds ids macOS never lists in `getDisplayModes` (NullMoth: it builds the list before `setDetailedTimings`). ECC review (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), fixed: the wake work ran on the PM thread in the controller gate only, so a WindowServer system entry (`_extEntry`: tests `pagingState` in the system + controller gates, opens the controller gate, sleeps on `fServerConnect` in the system gate) could miss the wakeup between its test and its sleep, and DidWake/DidPowerOn went out without the system gate (the family's order is system, then controller: ABBA risk) - now `getGraphicsSystemWorkLoop()->runAction` then the controller command gate, a re-check of the sleep inside both (`rd3fb_wake_recheck`), the wakeup through the controller gate (not gate specific; IOFramebuffer.cpp does the same); `test_wake_gates` explores every interleaving (controller-only loses the wakeup, the family order never does), so no delayed second wakeup was added. The gate is made at start, taken retained under `fLock` only while not stopping, removed at stop; a wake work that cannot run is counted (`wake_fails/retries/gave_up`) and retried by a thread call 50 ms apart (5 in a row, drained at stop). Paging already on at the first power-on after a sleep clears the sleep and its `WillSleep(false)` count (`wake_paging_already`, `wake_dropped`: no stale DidWakes later). `validateDetailedTiming` with `rdna3fbdtmap=0` answers Unsupported for every shape, every refusal counted (`rd3fb_dt_answer`). `open()` checks it runs in IOGraphics' system gate (the reads of `opened` / `gOpenReenter` rely on it) and takes it otherwise (`open_ungated`); a retry after a failed open is logged and `RDNA3,OpenOrder` says `open FAILED`. The IOFBIntegrated A/B is re-checked every ~2 s (re-applied, `integ_reset`; an open not through our override applied late, `integ_late`); `RDNA3,IntegratedAB` reads `never opened` until applied. The cursor's first image refused (`cursor_image_fails`) and unconverted images (`cursor_convert_fails`) are logged and counted. `RDNA3,Switches` publishes the switches as asked plus what is in effect (`cursorasync_active`, `hwcursor`, `vbl_call`, `vblonce_active`, `waketap`, `wakegate`); `RDNA3,FBStats` gains `wake_tap_events`, `wake_slept_pending`, `vbl_call_failed`, `open_*`, `integ_*` and is published after a wake, a cursor failure and at stop. Triage: the boot-args' `rdna3<switch>=N` against the published value, switches not in effect, the IOFBIntegrated property against the switch, MISSING FBStats keys, no VBL on a DCN head, the deaf tap / pending sleep / failed wake work, an unused or refused cursor, the AGDC vendor set but never asked / asked without AGDC armed / not the boot-arg's, and a TESTS-ONLY report's AGDC rows (`tests/test_triage_fb_n40.sh`). | N41 | Grey / stale windows, windows that appear only as a flash, black window captures (the owner's symptoms; traces of card runs 2026-10-17d / 17c: the VRAM of surfaces the Metal plugin had imported as BOs was freed under the live surface 64 / 83 times and came back at another address 13 / 19 times, so the family, the dirty mark and every page-off moved to VRAM nobody wrote, while the plugin's BO kept the first allocation through `RD3K_VRAM_FN_REF`; and a CPU lock of a surface WindowServer's GPU still writes read the system copy, because the plugin marks VRAM newest only after GPU completion). Done (NullMoth comparison: 47ec8d3 / 6372823 parking, 79f47d0 / 40daaa6 / e75be1e pending marks; written for this driver): `common/rdna3_vram_park.h` with `tests/test_vram_park.c` (park / reuse / grow / free, the 17d pattern replayed over a fake allocator: the family's address stays the plugin's with parking, moves without; no leak, no double release); `RD3VidMemory::deallocPhysical` parks, `allocPhysical` hands the parked allocation back when it fits (the same generation `fAllocGen`: same memory, same contents, so the IOP flip's `fPluginWroteGen` marker of N38 still holds; a new allocation after a grow gets a new one), `RD3VidMemory::free()` (new) releases the park and a live allocation the family left (`vm_free_live`: before N41 such VRAM was never given back), a dirty mark of a parked surface makes exactly that allocation live again first (`IOAccelVidMemory::wire`, else the mark is refused), a parked allocation stays in every table the free path cleans (present queue, latch, swap copy, write generations: still allocated) and leaves them when really released (`rd3AccelVidmemGoneAt` with the parked address); the aperture descriptor is rebuilt when the VRAM moved (`vm_apdesc_moved`); `debug.rdna3accel_vm_sticky` / `rdna3vmsticky` (1), counters `vm_parked`, `_unparked`, `_park_released`, `_park_grow`, `_reparked`, `_parked_bytes`, `_free_live`, `_released(_bytes)`, `_restored`, `_restore_miss`, `_dirty_moved` (a dirty mark of an allocation other than the surface's last BO import). `common/rdna3_surf_pending.h` with `tests/test_surf_pending.c` (marks counted per surface across command buffers, a write never completed costs one bounded wait, a full table drops the oldest); WindowServer's pending / ready marks through the dirty escape's plane flags (`RD3K_SS_PLANE_GPU_MARK`, uapi `RD_XNU_SURFACE_BO_MARK`, libdrm_xnu `drm_xnu_surface_mark`; only WindowServer's are taken), the page-off and the IOP zero-copy flip wait for them (bound `debug.rdna3accel_surface_wait_ms`, 100), the try-lock dirty mark (`RD_XNU_SURFACE_BO_DIRTY_TRY`, `RD3K_SS_BUSY`) so the plugin never waits for the accelerator's lock on the thread whose ready marks a page-off waits for; `debug.rdna3accel_surface_wait` / `rdna3surfwait` (1), counters `surface_wait_*`, `surface_dirty_trybusy`; the plugin (WindowServer only, `NVMTL_SURFACE_WAIT=0` off) marks the surfaces a command buffer writes pending and newest at commit and ready after the last part writing each, and keeps the old order when the kernel refuses (old kext: for good; waits off: retried each second); trace VMALLOC / VMFREE flags 16 / 32 / 64 and PAGEOFF wait flags, triage `VRAM parking` / `surface wait` rows and H-VMMOVE / H-VMPARK / H-SURFWAIT (`tests/test_triage_park.py`). ECC review (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), all confirmed findings fixed: the IOP flip waited after looking up the surface's VRAM (a park / grow / free in the wait left a stale handle): the wait comes first; a pending mark is taken only for a surface the caller can look up (`surface_wait_notown`); a late ready after a timeout took a newer command buffer's count: the dropped marks are kept as forgiven in `rd3_sp` and a ready takes those first (host test of that sequence, failing before); a waiter whose surface was cleared under it is `RD3_SPW_CLEARED`, not a successful wait (`surface_wait_cleared`); IOP flip waits counted and traced apart (`surface_wait_iop_*`, PAGEOFF arg 2 flag 4); `surface_wait_busy` gauge; the aperture descriptor kept only over exactly what the vidmem holds (`rd3_vp_apdesc_keep`); the escape's plane not rewritten; an older park released by address only; a refused VRAM release logged and counted (`vm_release_failed`); the dead `RD3AccelSurfaceWait` property removed; the boot line says MOOT with `rdna3a3ws=0`; free() traces `RD3_TRVM_OBJ_GONE` (no false H-VMMOVE on a reused object address); the plugin backs off on every pending-mark refusal but EBUSY, names every gate, logs stats, and marks a surface newest (try-lock) before its ready when the commit could not; triage reads WindowServer's plugin from `24-log-coredisplay.txt`, MOOT / parking / lost-ready / cleared / missing-key verdicts, SUMMARY rows; collect.sh keeps the boot lines in their own budget. `RD3VidMemory::free()` stays: `-fapple-kext` compiles `IOAccelVidMemory::free()` into a call through `__ZTV16IOAccelVidMemory` at OSObject::free's slot (Apple's own free of that class, what vtable patching gave this class before), checked by `tools/check_accel_slots.py` (with `RD3Resource::free` / `RD3Surface::free`, which do the same). ECC review round 2 (cpp-reviewer) and security review, all confirmed findings fixed: a lost ready made every later page-off of its surface wait the full bound (the next write's own ready took the forgiven mark, the write's count stayed, the next timeout forgave it again): such a pending mark is a ghost and the next timeout drops it for good, forgiven marks age out after `RD3_SP_FORGIVE_MS` (1 s), so a ready that never comes costs at most two bounded waits (`surface_wait_ghosts`, `_aged`; `lost_ready_bounded` host test: 8 timeouts in 8 page-offs before, 2 after); any process named WindowServer could hold page-offs on the family lock with its own pending marks: a mark is taken only from a process named WindowServer that runs as `_windowserver` (uid 88) or is the display client (the pid of its legacy swaps / display-pipe transactions), readies included (`rd3_sp_mark_gate`, `surface_wait_notreal`, `_ws_pid`, `_mark_pid`, `_mark_uid`; the boot line and the first mark taken name it), pending marks are refused as MOOT (ENXIO, the plugin's back-off) while no WindowServer surface can be VRAM (`rdna3a3ws=0`, `rdna3a3display=0`, no IOP flip; `surface_wait_moot`), and a timeout budget stops the waits after 4 timeouts with no real ready until one comes (`RD3_SPW_SKIP`, `surface_wait_starved`, `_skipped`, PAGEOFF flag 16); parked VRAM was never given back under pressure: only an allocation a GEM BO was imported over is parked (`RD3VidMemory::fBoGen`, `vm_park_noimport`), an `allocPhysical` refusal releases every park no BO holds any more (the core's `rdna3VramRefs` count, new) and asks once more, the parked bytes are capped (`debug.rdna3accel_vm_park_cap_mb`, 512: the oldest parks no BO holds go), counters `vm_park_trim_pressure`, `_trim_cap`, `_trimmed(_bytes)`, `_trim_held`, `_trim_unknown`, `vm_alloc_retry_ok` / `_fail`, VMFREE v2 `RD3_TRVMV2_NOBO` (the import ends there: no H-VMMOVE); the plugin counts the readies sent at a command buffer's end (its stats line, triage INFO). ECC review round 3 (cpp-reviewer), all confirmed findings fixed: the display client was recorded for any process named WindowServer before the swap / transaction was validated and replaced by the last caller (a renamed process passed the mark gate with one swap; a log line per pid flip): it is recorded only for a legacy swap that names our VRAM or a display-pipe transaction that drives one of our heads, the first one is sticky while it lives (`rd3_sp_display_client`: replaced only when no process has its pid or it is no longer named WindowServer, `proc_name`), a second one is refused and counted (`surface_wait_ws_pid_refused`, triage WARN), both logs rate limited (host test `display_client_sticky`); `deallocPhysical` read `fPark` after dropping `gVmParkLock` (a trim in between cleared it and `vm_parked_bytes` went negative): the parked bytes are counted from the copy taken under the lock, before the unlock; the trim looked only at the oldest 128 parks (releasable parks behind 128 a BO holds were never found): it pages through the list (up to 8 pages), dropping the held / unknown ones from each page (`rd3_vp_keep_releasable`, host test `paged_trim`); the trim's log line for an unknown core count was not rate limited: first, then every 1024th; between the trim's unlink and its release the object may be re-backed or freed and its address reused, so `kVmForgetAll` could wipe a newer allocation's records: the trim forgets only the released address (`kVmForgetAddr`: nothing else holds it before the release), and the records keyed by the object are forgotten by the object itself at its next `allocPhysical` or `free()` (`RD3VidMemory::fParkTrimmedPhys`), while it is alive and before it is backed again. Open: (1) only WindowServer's surfaces are marked pending; by default (`rdna3a3ws=0`) WindowServer's inputs stay out of A3, so the plugin's marks only engage with `rdna3a3ws=1` / `rdna3a3display=1` (triage and the boot line say MOOT); an application's own surface captured while its GPU writes it still reads the previous frame (no black: parking keeps the right VRAM); (2) whether `IOAccelVidMemory::free` calls `deallocPhysical` is not known (the card's `vm_free_live` says); (3) parks a BO still holds are never released (releasing them frees nothing; they go with the BO's surface), parks of an older core kext without `rdna3VramRefs` are never released under pressure (by design: their holders are unknown; counted `vm_park_trim_unknown`, logged at the first trim and every 1024th with the core's answer, triage WARN `kept because the core's reference count was unknown` - closed as "log it at least"), and a park the trim releases from another vidmem uses that object's pointer as a key only for its trace (closed, checked 2026-10-23: `rd3VmRelease` forgets the address before it gives the VRAM back, so no other vidmem can hold that address when its records are forgotten; the object forgets its own records); closed 2026-10-23 (quick/low-items): a trim paged by count, so a park unlinked meanwhile let one be skipped until the next trim - pages now resume after the last listed park's time (`rd3_vp_cursor`, `tests/test_vram_park.c` cursor_pages); (4) closed by the forgiven marks and the ghosts; closed 2026-10-23 (quick/low-items): an evicted surface's marks (or a reused forgiven-only slot's) are kept in a ring of 16 (`rd3_sp_evict_*`): its late readies take them (LATE), a new pending mark carries the rest in as forgiven marks aged from the eviction (`surface_wait_evict_kept`, triage's eviction WARN, `tests/test_surf_pending.c` evicted_marks_kept). Still open, needs a decision and the plugin (a per-write token in the marks: ABI change, Metal plugin built on the macOS runner): the table cannot tell which write a ready belongs to, so a ready that takes a forgiven mark while a newer write is pending is ambiguous (the late ready, or the newer write's own): the wait then ends at the next ready (later, never earlier), and a lost ready costs at most two bounded waits, not one; if both the dropped write's late ready and the newer write's ready were lost, the newer write's late ready after the second timeout finds no forgiven mark and can take a third write's count; the timeout budget counts timeouts in a row with no real ready, not per second (bound-independent: a 1 s bound would never trip a per-second count); (5) the ownership check uses `IOSurfaceRoot::lookupSurface` on the pending-mark path without the family lock, assuming no page-off holds IOSurfaceRoot's lock while it waits (only pending marks look up; readies never block on it); (6) one refused pending mark (e.g. ENOENT for one released surface) switches the plugin to the old order for a second for every surface; (7) when both try-lock newest marks of a surface find the accelerator's lock taken, its ready still goes first (counted: the plugin's stats line, triage WARN). Card must show: `vm sticky: 1` and `surface wait: 1` at boot; windows no longer grey / stale / flashing; `vm_parked` close to `vm_unparked`, `vm_dirty_moved` and `vm_restore_miss` 0, H-VMMOVE absent; screenshots / window captures not black; with `rdna3a3ws=1` `surface_wait_pending` = `_ready`, `_timeouts` 0; A/B `sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_vm_sticky=0` brings the symptoms back | accel, common, uapi, kext, libdrm_xnu, plugin, tools, triage, docs | L | done (host tests); card run open |
| N42 | VBL and flip done from the DCN interrupts instead of polls (N28 / N35 (5): completion at flip done, amdgpu's page-flip event; NullMoth delivers vblank from the interrupt into a HIGH ONCE thread call). Done: `hw/dcirq.c` ports `to_dal_irq_source_dcn32`, the vblank / vupdate_no_lock / pflip rows of `irq_source_info_dcn32`, `dal_irq_service_set` / `_set_generic` / `_ack` / `_ack_generic` / `dal_irq_service_to_irq_source` (`dc_interrupt_set` / `dc_interrupt_ack` for DC_IRQ_SOURCE_VBLANKn = OTG VSTARTUP, VUPDATEn = VUPDATE_NO_LOCK, PFLIPn = HUBP flip; src_ids 0x3C / 0x57 / 0x4F of `irqsrcs_dcn_1_0.h` on client DCE); **the HPD src_id fixed** (B7 had 0x2a, a DPP6 perfmon source: `DCN_1_0__SRCID__DC_HPD1_INT` is 9, so no HPD IV was ever claimed); `tests/sim_dc_irq.inc` models the sources as level interrupts through the IH (disabled silent, set acks first, a vblank per frame per head with two heads independent, the ack re-arms, a held flip raises PFLIP at the vblank that latches it, a storm cut); `common/rdna3_vbl_irq.h` + `tests/test_vbl_irq.c` (the poll fallback after 3 silent frames, no double delivery, the storm window). The core (`kext/src/RDNA3DisplayIrq.cpp`): the bring-up's IH handler acks each DCN IV (`amdgpu_dm_irq_handler`), counts and calls the head's framebuffer through `rdna3DisplayIrq` (ABI version 1; sources enabled only while registered, MSI live and allowed; again after a wake, a mode-1 reset, a mode set; a storm (> 64 in 100 ms) cuts the source). The framebuffer: VBL from the vblank interrupt into a HIGH ONCE thread call, the OTG poll as the fallback after 3 silent frames (counted, logged), the poll and the interrupt never delivering at once; `rdna3FlipLatchWait` version 2 woken by the pflip interrupt and saying so (`irqFlags`, `irqLatUs`; the accelerator falls back to version 1 for an older framebuffer). Switches: `rdna3vblirq` (default 1; 0 = the poll alone, as before), `debug.rdna3_vblirq` (runtime off / on, forgets storms), `rdna3hpdirq` (default 0: HPD interrupts counted and logged only; 1 = B7's connector probe). Counters: `debug.rdna3_dispirq` / `RDNA3,DisplayIrq`, `RDNA3,HPDIrqs`, `RDNA3,FBStats` virq_* / latch_*, `swaps_pq_irqdone / irqlatus / irqlatmax / irqlive / fbwaitv1_h<N>`; triage `vblank irq`, `display irq`, present-queue N42 rows. **The card must show**: `display irq: head 0 (OTG n, HUBP n): the framebuffer registered for 0x3, enabled 0x3`, `debug.rdna3_dispirq` vblank / pflip counts growing with the frames, FBStats `virq_irqs` ~ `vbl_delivered`, `virq_fallback_runs` 0, `latch_irq_woke` close to `latch_waits`, `swaps_pq_irqdone` growing; no `interrupt storm` line; the desktop as smooth as with `rdna3vblirq=0` (the A/B). Open: the interrupt sources' behaviour on the card (level vs pulse, VSTARTUP_INT_TYPE left at its reset value as amdgpu does; whether MSI delivers them at 60-120 Hz); `dm_vupdate_high_irq`'s VRR path (VUPDATE decoded, not requested); ODM heads' second pipe: decided 2026-10-23, no change - amdgpu enables and handles only one PFLIP source per CRTC (`IRQ_TYPE_PFLIP + otg_inst`, `dm_pflip_high_irq` maps it back by otg_inst) and programs both ODM pipes in the same flip, so the primary HUBP's flip done is the CRTC's; ours uses the head's primary pipe the same way; the trace event for interrupt-done latches: closed 2026-10-23 (quick/low-items) - the present queue's LATCHED event carries `RD3_TRP_IRQ_DONE` (0x40) with the interrupt's latency in v3, H-PRESENT counts them, the timeline says `(by the pflip interrupt, N us after it)`; the comment and framebuffer text of why 6 now name a head still lost as well as the whole display; `rdna3hpdirq=1` (B7's probe on an HPD interrupt) never run on the card. **ECC review round 1** (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), fixed: after a wake or mode-1 reset that did not bring the DCN back (power lost and rdna3resume=0 / its resume failed; heads down and not restored) no OTG / HUBP interrupt register is written any more - the sources are forgotten as off (`displayIrqResync`, `rd3_virq_wake_dcn_up` / `rd3_virq_reset_dcn_up`, why 6 `RD3K_DISPIRQ_WHY_DOWN`, `down= downs= downivs=`) until a later wake / reset brings it back; the MSI verification turning `fIrqLive` off now turns the sources off (`msi off` sync, why 2); the storm guard keeps at most 4 IVs that follow a quiet stretch of 50 ms (within 10 ms) out of its window and counts them (`drained= drains=`; a working source cannot build a backlog - it raises once per ack, `sim_dc_irq.inc` stalls 100 frames and finds one IV - so a storm after a stall is cut at most 4 IVs later; the IV's own IH timestamp is not used, its clock is not established here); the framebuffer's stop waits for a REGISTER in flight (counted under fLock with fStopping, the wake work's pattern) before it unregisters and unregisters again a registration found after the timers are drained (`virq_late_unreg`; `test_vbl_irq` explores every interleaving, the order before failing); DCE IVs no handler claims (`dceother=`, the first four `dce=src:ext:ring`), HPD6 / HPD6 RX IVs (`hpdnopin=`, `RDNA3,HPDIrqsNoPin`), unrouted IVs per kind and instance (`unr= unrinst=`), bad instances (`badmask=`), failed acks / disables (`ackfails= offfails=`) are counted, logged (rate-limited) and WARN in triage's `display irq` row, which now reads the live sysctl before the property copy (republished at every HPD IV) and takes `RDNA3,HPDIrqs`; `vblank irq` says `never attempted` with the reason (`virq_noreg`) and gives a framebuffer without a DCN head an INFO row; the present queue's pflip row says `no latch wait yet` instead of `not live` before any latch. Open after round 2: a head still down keeps its stale enables until it is up (no register of a down head is written; its IVs are acked as `unrouted`); whether a restore on the card ever leaves one head of two lost (the per-head path is host-tested only). | hw, kext, fb, accel, common, tests, tools, triage, docs | L | done (sim + host tests); card run open |

(1) The Metal plugin's buffer texture alias: `vkCreateImage(buffer texture)` failed with `-1000158000` (`VK_ERROR_INVALID_DRM_FORMAT_MODIFIER_PLANE_LAYOUT_EXT`) about 32 times a second from WindowServer: RADV takes an explicit linear row pitch only as a multiple of 256 B (`ac_surface_override_offset_stride` / `ac_surface_get_pitch_align`, the GFX10.3+ descriptor's 256 B pitch unit), the plugin checked NVIDIA's 32 B; every successful alias in the logs had pitch % 256 == 0, every failure not; task 19's private copy (48a2fe7) drew the texture at the cost of a new image and a synchronous GPU upload per use. Done (host tests; the plugin is not built here, `metal/` needs `build.yml full: true`): `metal/plugin/nvmtl_linear_pitch.h` (`nvmtl_linear_pitch_ok`, `tests/test_linear_pitch.c`: passes 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 2304, 2560, 7680, fails 64, 128, 192, 320, 384, 576, 960, per bytes per element), used by `nvmtl_vk_image_create_buffer_alias` (DRM-modifier path only) before `vkCreateImage`, so `nvt_alloc_fail` no longer fires for it, and by the IOSurface alias (`NVT_WHY_PITCH`, now 256); the reason is logged and counted (`buffer texture copy (pitch): ...`, first 8 then every 1024th, `nvmtl_vk_buftex_pitch_copies()`, also in the buffer texture census line); triage `buffer texture copy (pitch)` INFO with the count (WARN for a plugin older than N43), the plugin trace shows an older plugin's `-1000158000` as `INFO buftex-pitch`, not `FAIL alloc-fail` (`tests/test_triage_n43.py`, `tests/test_plugin_trace.sh`); `rdna3_vktest` checks the rule on the card (`LINPITCH:` line: explicit linear row pitch 64 -> `-1000158000`, 256 -> success; confirmed with the host RADV in the loopback), triage `linear pitch`. (2) UCLK at 96 MHz is the idle floor in every report (UCLK DPM on, levels 96 / 456 / 772 / 1218, Linux idles at 96 too, `umc` activity 0-2 %, no memory load ever measured). UCLK STAYED` is the finding: the PMFW's UCLK DPM does not follow the load). | N44 | WindowServer never uses IOPresentment transactions with our driver (N39: page-flip mode, `iop_ok` 0 on every card run) because CoreDisplay classifies every IOFramebuffer's GPU vendor by its class name. Probe runs 10 and the cfstr peek (macOS 15.8.1 / 24H32, the card VM's build): `CoreDisplay::UseIOPresentment()` requires every framebuffer to be a known vendor; `_GetGPUVendorForFramebufferService` (0x7FF80412CC38) takes `IOObjectCopyClass(fb)` - the LEAF C++ class name, not the service name - and does four case-insensitive `CFStringFind`s in order: `Intel` -> 2, `AMD` -> 4, `AppleParavirt` -> 0x10, `NVDA` -> 8, none -> 1 (unknown); `___GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke` ORs them over all IOFramebuffers and clears bit 1, so `RDNA3Framebuffer` gave gpuVendors 0 and useIOP was forced off for the session. Every card report shows it: `GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke: 0x1000002d1 1 RDNA3Framebuffer IOService:/...` for both heads (NullMoth's class names contain NVDA). The switches are `GetBoolCFPreference` lookups in the `com.apple.CoreDisplay` domain; CoreDisplay's static values: `useIOP` 1, `useIOP_onIG` 1, `useIOP_onNVIDIA` 0, `useIOP_onAMD` 0, `useIOP_onAPV` 0 (none set on the runner; whether `InitializeUserDefaults` changes them at run time is not known - NullMoth works with onNVIDIA statically 0). Done: (1) IOKit instantiates the leaf `AMDRDNA3Framebuffer : RDNA3Framebuffer` (`RDNA3_FB_CLASS` in `fb/rdna3-fb.cpp`, both IOClass strings in `fb/Info.plist`; `fb/Makefile` refuses a bundle whose IOClass differs and `make check` looks for its metaclass; `tests/test_fb_class.py` keeps source, plist, Makefile and triage equal and the name free of `Intel` / `AppleParavirt` / `NVDA` and of Apple's class names). The service name stays `RDNA3Framebuffer` (setName), so ioreg paths, `serviceMatching("RDNA3Framebuffer")` in the accelerator and AGDC and `ioreg -c RDNA3Framebuffer` in the collectors (a subclass matches) are unchanged. Logged at start: `RDNA3-fb: head N class AMDRDNA3Framebuffer, name RDNA3Framebuffer: CoreDisplay classifies the framebuffer by this class name (contains AMD: vendor 4 expected, IOPresentment allowed)` (`-- NOT AMDRDNA3Framebuffer ...` when IOClass is not the leaf). (2) The VM writes `useIOP` and `useIOP_onAMD` = true AnyUser/AnyHost (`/Library/Preferences/com.apple.CoreDisplay`) on every boot, the install boot included (`autoreport.sh` `coredisplay_iop_prefs`); WindowServer reads them at its start, so they count from the next boot; `rdna3iopprefs=0` in the step's boot-args takes them out (rdna3vm passes it to the install boot too); the tests file says what this boot's WindowServer started with (`== CoreDisplay prefs ...`), `collect.sh` packs `19-coredisplay-prefs.txt` (`tests/test_coredisplay_prefs.sh`). Triage `present mode, GPU vendor` per framebuffer: PASS vendor 4; INFO `IOPresentment off: framebuffer classed vendor 1 (unknown)` for the old class (old reports); FAIL for the new class still at 1 or another class (another driver's framebuffer; the kext's own `-- NOT` line is the `fb class` FAIL); WARN other vendors, no line, no log; `present mode, CoreDisplay prefs` INFO with useIOP_onAMD at WindowServer's start, WARN when unknown or not written; the `planes in transactions` FAIL names the vendor-1 classes. The card run must show: `GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke: ... 4 AMDRDNA3Framebuffer` for both heads, `CoreDisplay prefs ... started with useIOP_onAMD 1`, then `debug.rdna3accel_iop_ok` > 0 (and `init_page_flip` no longer `on`). Open: (a) which user/host the preference lookup uses is not proven: if both heads show 4 and useIOP_onAMD 1 but transactions stay off, write the keys into the `_windowserver` user's domain too (triage prints this lead as INFO); (b) a new framebuffer binary needs the user's approval in macOS (`rdna3vm approve`); (c) CoreDisplay may take other AMD-only paths for a vendor-4 framebuffer (watch the CoreDisplay log for AMD-specific errors); (d) `kern.boottime` was parsed with a greedy `.*sec = ` that captured `usec` (`prune_old_traces` in autoreport.sh, `collect_plugin_trace.sh`, the boot-start record): fixed here (anchored `^{ *sec = `, `tests/test_prune_traces.sh`, `tests/test_plugin_trace.sh`); an unparseable boot time is said (`boot time unknown`, prefs record `could NOT tell`), (e) `rdna3iopprefs=0` reaches no install boot when the bundle is current (rdna3vm returns early): keys left by an earlier boot then show as `WindowServer started with useIOP_onAMD 1` and triage WARNs `this run is NOT the A/B without them` (the run after takes them out); the kext's `-- NOT AMDRDNA3Framebuffer` line is a triage FAIL (`present mode, fb class`) | fb, tools, tests, docs | S | done (host tests); card run open |
| N45 | Lessons from Navi48-MacOS (https://github.com/Almosst-DEV/Navi48-MacOS, commit d1f7bad, RX 9070 XT on macOS 26.6.2 Tahoe; MIT top level, `src/navi48-bringup` BSD-3 with its own NOTICE, `third-party/metal2vulkan-n48` LGPL-3.0: do not take from it; credit Almosst-DEV in comments and NOTICE for anything ported). All their addresses are from macOS 26, ours is 15.8.1 (24H32): every item is an A/B on the card. Ranked: (1) CoreDisplay's `GetBoolCFPreference` reads `CFPreferencesCopyValue(key, "com.apple.CoreDisplay", kCFPreferencesAnyUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost)` = the ByHost store (`tools/pc/cdpref.c`), keys read by `_CGXMappedDisplayStart`: `useIOP`, `useIOP_onAMD`, `displaySurfaceUsesIOSurface`, `useIOP_gamma_onAMD`; N44 wrote only AnyHost (`/Library/Preferences/com.apple.CoreDisplay`); DONE (host tests, card run open): `autoreport.sh` writes `useIOP` / `useIOP_onAMD` also into the ByHost file `/Library/Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.CoreDisplay.<IOPlatformUUID>` (by path, no `-currentHost`: as root it would write /var/root/Library/Preferences/ByHost, the wrong store; UUID from `ioreg IOPlatformExpertDevice`, refused when it holds anything but hex and `-`), each store reported on its own (`AnyHost written; ByHost(<uuid>) could NOT be written (...)`), both taken out with `rdna3iopprefs=0`; `collect.sh` packs both stores and `ls -l ByHost/` into `19-coredisplay-prefs.txt`; triage `CoreDisplay prefs` reports both stores and WARNs when only AnyHost held the keys at WindowServer's start. Only these two keys are written; `displaySurfaceUsesIOSurface` / `useIOP_gamma_onAMD` are not (their effect is unknown: the next A/B). Still open: confirm the user/host on 15 with a probe of `GetBoolCFPreference` (WIP patch `docs/handoff/wip-probe-boolpref.patch`, unreviewed, not applied). RDNA3BoMemory now overrides both with kIOReturnUnsupported and a log line (`RDNA3: GEM: RDNA3BoMemory::doMap called (N such): ...`, first 8 then every 1024th; triage `BO map` FAIL, host test `tests/test_triage_bomem.py`); the core's imports, checked as object files against every x86_64 config/*.exports of both tags, are all exported now, and CI's `KPI guard for the core` blocks. Found on the way and fixed (2026-10-25, card run open): for the same reason the IOMemoryMap did not hold RDNA3BoMemory either (it held the parent, fVramDev / fDoorbellDev), and IOUserClient::mapClientMemory64 releases the descriptor right after createMappingInTask, so the BO reference was dropped as soon as the map was made: a client could close a VRAM or doorbell BO (GEM_CLOSE or closing the device) with its CPU mapping in place and read and write that VRAM or doorbell after it went to another process (the A3 security fix did not hold). Now, the amdgpu way (drm_gem_mmap_obj's GEM reference held by the VMA): every VRAM / doorbell BO has one RDNA3BoMemory that holds a BO reference for its whole life and maps itself (makeMapping on itself, doMap / doUnmap forwarded to an IOMemoryDescriptor::withAddressRange of exactly the BO's BAR range), so each IOMemoryMap holds the BO until xnu frees it, which unmaps first; the map outlives GEM_CLOSE, clientClose (destroyUserReferences moves the mappings set to the connection port) and the file, and goes with IOConnectUnmapMemory64 or the task's last send right. The BO links to it weakly (rdna3_bo::os_map under fBoMapLock, a leaf lock), so a second map and IOConnectUnmapMemory64's kIOMapReference lookup find the same descriptor; its taggedRelease clears the link before the last reference goes (common/rdna3_bo_map.h, rd3_bomap_release_unlinks; OSObject::taggedTryRetain is xnu-private). The map path also refuses userptr and no-CPU-access BOs (amdgpu_gem_object_mmap; every userptr BO had mmap offset 0, which the old libdrm_xnu's unmap reached). libdrm_xnu unmapped with memory type 0, which named no BO, so every unmap failed and mappings piled up until the device closed: it now passes the BO's offset, and an address mapped twice goes with its last unmap. Counters debug.rdna3_vram `bomap_live` / `bomap_live_kib` / `bomap_made` / `bomap_reused` / `bomap_refused` / `bomap_nobo` / `bomap_fail`, log lines `GEM: CPU map of offset 0x... refused: ...` and `GEM: CPU map|unmap of a ... BO ... failed in xnu`, triage `BO map` rows and the VRAM heap's INFO line; host tests `tests/test_bo_map.c` (GEM_CLOSE, file close, shared BO, doorbell, refusals against the real UAPI), `tests/test_triage_bomem.py`, the loopback backend refuses an unmap whose offset names no mapped BO (`tests/test_libdrm_amdgpu.c`). ECC review round 1 (security + C++ reviewers; nothing CRITICAL/HIGH introduced), fixed: BOs held only by mappings were bounded by no limit (map + GEM_CLOSE loops, close and reopen): each descriptor is charged to the uid of the file that made it (rdna3_drm_file.owner, `rdna3_drm_bomap_charge` / `_uncharge`, dev->bomap per uid), capped at RD3_BOMAP_DEVICE_MAX (65536) descriptors on the device and, for any uid but root / _windowserver, RD_MAX_HANDLES descriptors and twice the visible VRAM (`rd3_bomap_quota_admit`), and the IOSurface import quota counts the surface VRAM the owner's mappings hold (each BO once, a reopened file still sees it); refusals logged with the reason, `bomap_quota`; the client's map flags no longer pick the cache mode (doMap forces write-combined for VRAM, uncached for doorbells); withBo's causes are logged with the physical range (`no descriptor for the ...`, `bomap_nodesc`); drmFree sets a flag under fBoMapLock and waits for releases in flight, a release after the teardown leaves its BO and logs (`went after the DRM teardown`); libdrm_xnu keeps a mapping's entry when the unmap fails (a retry no longer reaches libc's munmap) and drops it when the connection goes; `tests/test_bo_map_race.c` runs the release protocol under 8 threads. ECC review round 2 (security; nothing CRITICAL/HIGH), fixed: the charge was a flag on the BO, so a descriptor made between the previous one's unlink (taggedRelease) and its uncharge (drmBoMapRelease) went uncharged, outside every cap; the charge is now the descriptor's own (`rdna3_bomap_charge`: generation, size, uid, foreign, exempt; made whenever a descriptor is made, given back exactly), and the BO keeps only the newest charge's generation for file_foreign_bytes' per-BO view; root and _windowserver take no slot of the 64-uid table (they could have been refused TABLE_FULL); each quota reason's first refusal is logged on its own limiter (`refused by the quota for the first time`); the race test now charges and uncharges under the outer lock and checks the uid's count against the live descriptors. Still open: (b1, HIGH impact per the security review, no cheap complete fix) a task can mach_vm_remap its own mapping (copy = FALSE), share it into a fork with VM_INHERIT_SHARE, or make a memory entry of it (mach_make_memory_entry): each references the device pager's VM object, which populateDevicePager fills for the whole range at map time and xnu keeps populated after the IOMemoryMap is unmapped, so it survives the BO and reaches whatever the range holds next; clearing VRAM on allocation protects only the initial contents, and IOMemoryDescriptor::redirect blocks only pages not yet populated. Research direction (needs a card run): map with kIOMapUnique and take the pages away on the BO's end with IOMemoryMap::redirect (UPL_BLOCK_ACCESS / upl_transpose), as IOFramebuffer does for its VRAM; (b2, LOW) a BO kept alive only by a mapping is in no file's handle table, so rdna3resume's S3 eviction (rdna3_drm_device_for_each_vram_bo) does not save its contents (the range stays owned); (b3, LOW) a kext and a userspace of different builds: an old libdrm_xnu's unmaps still fail, and its freed BOs now stay allocated until the process closes the device (triage `BO map` WARN for offset 0) - install both from one `full: true` build; (b4) teardown: done in round 1 (above); what stays is that a BO whose mapping outlives drmFree is leaked, logged; (c) the guard's parser knows 26.4.1's output format only: the first CI run on the macOS 15 runner confirms it reads that one too (an unknown line is an error, not a pass); (d) the xnu lists are of xnu-12377.101.15: regenerate them (`gen_xnu_kpi_lists.py --tag <new>`) when the VM's macOS moves on ECC round 2 (113d5f8), LOW, open: (e) a drain does not refuse arms, so an HPD storm with rdna3hpdirq=1 and rdna3hotplug=0 keeps displayShutdown / displayMirrorOff / hotplugSuspend waiting until it ends (logged every 30 s): a stop flag checked in displayHotplug, or teardownInterrupt before displayMirrorOff; (f) `runner` holds only the last thread to enter: self-stop detection is reliable only on ONCE calls (no call site stops from its own run); (g) a refused self-free does not set `stopping` or cancel what is queued (no call site does it); (h) older than N47: statsStart (from displayWakeWork) and statsStop (displayWillSleep) are not serialized - fWakeCall is not drained before the sleep's statsStop.
