| N48 | Card run 2026-10-25c, Metal plugin on macOS 26.4.1 (Chess, WindowServer, Terminal). (1) Chess (OpenGL over the plugin, apps mode) died deterministically (3 launches) right after `newTextureView: depth/stencil view format 260 aspect 6`, the `texturedFragment2DDepthResolve` pipeline and `G14 census: copy texture->buffer 971x680`. ROOT CAUSE, certain from the three crash reports (Chess-2026-10-25-043835.000.ips / 043835.ips / 043932.ips, copied from the Tahoe disk): EXC_CRASH SIGABRT, `__assert_rtn` in libvulkan_radeon `gfx_or_compute_copy_memory_to_image.cold.1` <- `radv_CmdCopyBufferToImage2` <- `vk_common_CmdCopyBufferToImage` <- RDNA3MTLDriver `nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_buffer_to_image_level_layer` +858 <- `-[NVMTLBlitCommandEncoder copyFromBuffer:...toTexture:...options:]` <- AppleMetalOpenGLRenderer `gldBlitFramebufferData` <- `gldReadFramebufferData` <- GLEngine `glReadPixels_Exec` <- Chess; in our Mesa (d381c361, src/amd/vulkan/meta/radv_meta_copy.c) that function starts with `assert(image->vk.samples == 1)`: Apple's GL renderer uploads a BUFFER into a 4-SAMPLE texture, which Vulkan does not allow (the guess of the first analysis, a texture->buffer copy, was wrong: that census line is only the copy before it; stock RADV 26.2.5 on the dev host does not assert on the image->buffer direction). Fixed in two steps. Step 1 (ff5cb13, host test `tests/test_copy_pack.c`, `metal/plugin/nvmtl_copy_pack.h`): both copy functions follow Vulkan valid usage (1 sample, one aspect the format has, depth/stencil element sizes - every depth image said 4 B -, depth/stencil buffer offset a multiple of 4 (`the copy is DROPPED` is in the log line), non-null VkImage/VkBuffer); a refusal is a log line with the numbers, the G14 census line gains vk format, samples, aspect, element size, Metal pixel format and region, the blit methods refuse objects that are not the plugin's, and the first copy shapes per direction are bracketed by `recording` / `layout transition recorded` / `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer recorded` lines (a copy that is multisample, depth/stencil or non-colour is always logged, 16 then every 256th). Step 2 (host test `tests/test_msupload.c`, `metal/plugin/nvmtl_msupload.h`, probe `tests/vk_msupload_probe.c` run on the dev host's RADV): the upload into a multisample COLOUR texture is carried instead of refused: a full-screen-triangle pass at the image's sample count (`nvmtl_msupload_spv.h`, glslc, spirv-val clean; pipelines cached per (view format, samples)) whose fragment shader reads the region's bytes from the buffer by device address (push constants: address, row, origin, size, byte order) and writes them, a covered pixel writes every sample; 8-bit RGBA/BGRA UNORM and sRGB (written through the UNORM twin view, so the bytes are copied, not converted), R8 and RG8; the probe checks 2/4/8 samples, sRGB, an unaligned buffer offset and the pixels around the region byte for byte after a resolve. Still refused with a log line (`copy_buffer_to_image: REFUSED - a N-sample image: ...`): multisample depth/stencil destinations (they need a depth-export variant of the pass), multisample arrays, other colour formats (RGBA16F, 10-bit ...). The texture->buffer direction of a multisample texture stays refused (Metal's blit cannot read one either) - WRONG: Apple's GL does exactly that for the stencil resolve, carried since N52. Triage (`tools/triage_n48.py`, test `tests/test_triage_n48.py`): `plugin copy refused`, `multisample upload`, `framebuffer fetch`, `plugin copy bracket`. (2) Framebuffer fetch: WindowServer and Terminal build fragment modules with a `[[color(0)]]` input (translator: input attachment at binding 192+n); the set layout has no such binding and no render pass an input attachment. Done: `nvmtl_spirv_fbfetch.h` scans the module (host test `tests/test_spirv_fbfetch.c`, patched module checked with spirv-val), one log line per pipeline (`vk: fbfetch: fragment module #N declares ...`, the first 8 of that literal, with counters read / declared-only / zeroed), and the experiment `NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1` (default off) that replaces each read by a zero vector. IMPLEMENTED in the follow-up (branch quick/fbfetch, host tests `tests/test_fbfetch_plan.c` / `tests/test_spirv_fbfetch.c`, probe `tests/vk_fbfetch_probe.c`, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode, NOT yet seen on the card). Card run 2026-10-25d (artifacts as rectangles over text cells with a red/green (u,v) gradient in almost every app, broken glyphs in Terminal/Maps): the translator's blend/glyph modules read `[[color(0)]]` (binding 192+n) from an undefined descriptor. Mechanism chosen: render-pass input attachments, because the plugin records with `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` and the stock RADV 26.2.5 on the dev host (same RX 7800 XT) offers `VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read` but NOT `VK_EXT_rasterization_order_attachment_access`, so the only other route would be rewriting every pass builder to dynamic rendering. Pipeline (`nvmtl_fbfetch_prepare`, `nvmtl_fbf_plan`): the fragment module is scanned before the render pass is built; each read of colour n is SERVED when the pipeline has a float colour attachment n (all 1-sample, non-tessellation pipelines), otherwise that read alone is replaced by a zero vector (a module declares colour inputs the pipeline has no attachment for: Terminal 0x7 declared, one attachment; integer formats, multisample targets, tessellation, `NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1` are zeroed, logged and counted per reason). `nvk_pipeline.fbread` = served mask. Render pass (`nvmtl_make_render_pass(p, load, fbin)`): colour n is also `pInputAttachments[n]`, both references GENERAL, plus a by-region self-dependency (COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT/WRITE -> FRAGMENT_SHADER/INPUT_ATTACHMENT_READ); the variant key is load bits | fbin << 16 (`nvmtl_rp_variant`), so a pass without input attachments is byte-for-byte what it was. The subpass-input variables are moved to descriptor set 3 (`nvmtl_spirv_fbfetch_set`, before the 0 -> 1 retarget; the pipeline layout of such pipelines has 4 sets, an empty layout in the heap's slot when the device has no heap), an INPUT_ATTACHMENT set of its own (bindings 192..199, fragment): set 1's dirty tracking/reuse/copy logic is untouched. Per pass, at the first draw that reads (`nvmtl_fbf_prepare_pass`): a 2D view per input attachment (level/slice/format of the attachment, usage INPUT_ATTACHMENT) retired with the command buffer, one set from a dedicated pool (freed with the other sets), written once; per draw (`nvmtl_fbf_bind`, from `nvmtl_vk_cmd_bind_set`): pipeline barrier (same masks, by region) + bind set 3. Colour render targets now get `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_INPUT_ATTACHMENT_BIT` next to COLOR_ATTACHMENT (not 3D volumes; the placement size query uses the same bits). Encoder (`-nvmtlFetchPass:` in NVMTLObjects.m, called for the pipeline actually drawn, sampler variants included): a pass begins with the first pipeline's inputs; a later pipeline that reads an attachment the pass lacks ENDS the pass and BEGINS it again with LOAD and the union of the inputs (the mesh stage's restart; the visibility query is closed and reopened), counted and logged (`vk: fbfetch: ... RESTARTED`). Never a silent undefined read: a pass whose attachment cannot be an input (layered pass, 3D slice, no usage, view failure, descriptor-set allocation failure, a pass that lacks the input) SKIPS the draw (`c->nobind`) with a counted log line. Probe result on the dev host's RADV (stock Mesa 26.2.5; the plugin's Mesa d381c361 was not fetched/compared): 1920x1080 (DCC on) B8G8R8A8 UNORM/SRGB (sRGB view; UNORM view over an sRGB image), R8G8B8A8, RGBA16F, RGBA32F: gradient then a fetch draw, 0 wrong of 8.3 M values; 40 overlapping fetch draws that add 0.01 each in RGBA32F: exact (0 wrong pixels), and 333959 wrong pixels with the barrier removed (NOBARRIER=1 control, so the test does bite); restart shape (pass A without inputs, pass B LOAD with inputs, same framebuffer): exact. Still UNSUPPORTED (zero vector + counted log line, `UNSUPPORTED (reason)`): multisample colour targets (needs an MS image type + `Sample` operand in the module), integer colour formats, layered passes, slices of 3D textures, tessellation pipelines, imageblock/tile-shader inputs (bindings 200..479 are not touched by this work). Review round 1 (ECC: cpp-reviewer approved, silent-failure-hunter 3 HIGH, fixed): nvlog keeps the first 8 calls per FORMAT LITERAL, so every refusal reason and every skip cause has a literal of its own, zeroing/scan/move failures are logged unconditionally, a SUMMARY line (`nvlog_uncapped`, key=value, <= every 10 s, powers of two of error counters, at exit) carries all totals and triage reads the newest (`nvmtl_log_cap.h`, host test `tests/test_fbfetch_plan.c` simulates the cap); a skipped fetch draw is counted (`c->skipped`) and traced `NOT DRAWN`; the severest reason is reported; a restart keeps LOAD for good. Open / watch: (a) render-pass compatibility: a pipeline's own pass (`p->rp`) and the begun variant can differ in their input-attachment lists (strictly the spec wants compatible passes; benign on RADV 26.2.5, WATCH on d381c361); (b) write-after-read: an ordinary draw after a fetch draw on the same pixels has no barrier of its own; the probe's interleaved case (60 fetch/ordinary draws, RGBA32F) is exact on 26.2.5. Risks: it is unverified on the card and on the plugin's own Mesa build (GENERAL colour layout + self-dependency + a barrier per fetching draw cost performance; a stale `rpx` ceiling of 1020 variants per pipeline is now shared with the input-attachment variants); `A8` render targets are read through an identity-swizzle R8 view (alpha-only fetch reads R). (3) Per-draw encode timing: trace record kind 17 (`NVT_K_ENC_SPLIT`) per render encoder that drew: pipeline lookup / descriptor writes / surface-in checks in us, draws, variants built on the draw, the slowest draw (cost with the trace off: a load and a branch per site); `tools/plugin_trace.py` prints it (`encoder cb N: D draw(s) in ...`, plus an `encode-time split` summary block; test in `tests/test_plugin_trace.sh`). Open: the card run with Chess (the next unknown, glReadPixels' result and the depth resolve pass - see `msupload:` and `plugin copy refused` rows), a multisample depth/stencil upload if the log names one | plugin, tests, docs | M | done (host tests, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode); card run open |
| N49 | Card runs 2026-10-25c..e (macOS 26.4.1): **Activity Monitor shows GPU 0 % for every process**, while the accelerator's PerformanceStatistics said `Device Utilization %` = 100 at idle. What Activity Monitor reads (read on the owner's 26.4.1 VM from the binaries, certain): GPU History = SafeEjectGPU.framework's `-[SafeEjectGPUSession getPercentUtilization]`, `PerformanceStatistics` / `Device Utilization %` of the GPU's IOAccelerator; the per-process `% GPU` / `GPU Time` = SafeEjectGPU's `apps_under_service`, which walks the accelerator's subtree and, for entries that conform to `IOAccelSubmitter2` (IOAccelContext2 and its 2D/GL/CL/video contexts, IOAccelCommandQueue; `IOAccelDevice2` only names the API, `IOGPUDeviceUserClient`'s `AppUsage` is the IOGPUFamily form), parses `IOUserClientCreator` (`pid %lu, %s`) and sums `accumulatedGPUTime` per pid, the newest `lastSubmittedTime` marking it active. The family creates both numbers in `IOAccelSubmitter2::start` and adds to them only from its block fences (`IOAccelBlockFence::notifyClient`), which our jobs (DRM bridge) never use. The kernel's per-task GPU time (`task_power_info_v2.gpu_energy.task_gpu_utilisation`, `ml_gpu_stat_update`) is fed only through `gpu_accumulate_time` (osfmk/kern/energy_perf.c), exported by com.apple.kpi.private alone in xnu-12377.101.15 (`ml_gpu_stat_update` by no KPI): Apple's Intel GPU kexts call it, AMD's and IOAcceleratorFamily2 do not; kmutil refuses it (N47), so not used. The SMU value: amdgpu's `gpu_busy_percent` is `METRICS_AVERAGE_GFXACTIVITY` (`smu_v13_0_0_get_smu_metrics_data` -> `AverageGfxActivity`, the table SMU 13.0.7 shares), which `hw/smu13.c` reads already; it is 100 % at idle on this card (P2), so it cannot be fixed by porting. Done: `common/rdna3_gpu_busy.h` (`tests/test_gpu_busy.c`): the kernel rings' busy time and the GPU time per process in amdgpu's fdinfo model (`amdgpu_ctx_fence_time`: finished - scheduled; scheduled = our submission, finished = `amdgpu_fence_process`'s fence read from the EOP interrupt, a waiter or the take; a job queued behind another starts at the previous one's end, so a ring's per-job times add up to its busy time); `gfx11_fence_read` / `sdma60_fence_read` (`amdgpu_fence_read`, sim-checked); the core takes it under a spinlock in `RDNA3Bringup::submit` / `fenceSignaled` / `handleInterrupt` and answers `RD3K_GPUBUSY_FN` (`rdna3GpuBusyTake`); the accelerator's 250 ms perfstats tick publishes `Device Utilization %` / `GPU Activity(%)` (any ring with work) and `Renderer` / `Tiler Utilization %` (the GFX ring) over the last second (`common/rdna3_gpu_util.h`), and adds each process's GPU time to the `accumulatedGPUTime` of its first IOAccelSubmitter2 under the accelerator (`OSNumber::addValue`, as the family does; `lastSubmittedTime` too); a core that does not answer: the SMU value as before, counted. Counters `debug.rdna3accel_perf_rings` / `_smu` / `_noanswer` / `_util` / `_utilgfx` / `_jobs` / `_flushed` / `_lostns` / `_gpuns` / `_nosubns` / `_nosub` / `_creditfail`; logs `perfstats: GPU time: pid N +X us ...`, `perfstats: pid N used the GPU ... has no IOAccelSubmitter2`, `perfstats: the core did not give its ring busy time`, the core's `GPU busy: ... flushed ...`; triage `gpu_util_verdict` + serial patterns (`tests/test_triage_latch.sh`). Open: (1) **a process with no IOAccelSubmitter2 showed 0 %**: in 2026-10-25e only WindowServer had one (two RD32DContext); Chess, Terminal, System Settings, Activity Monitor had IOAccelDevice2 + IOAccelSharedUserClient2 + the DRM bridge only. **Done in the plugin (host-tested, card run open)**: how a process gets an IOAccelCommandQueue, read on the owner's 26.4.1 VM (certain from the binaries): `IOGraphicsAccelerator2::newUserClient`'s switch maps type 8 to `newCommandQueue()` (0 surface, 1/3/7 contexts, 2 2D context, 4 display pipe, 5 IOAccelDevice2, 6 IOAccelSharedUserClient2, 9 GL drawable, 0x20 Metal surface, 0x21 memory info), the family's default `newCommandQueue` returns a plain IOAccelCommandQueue (our accelerator does not override it and needs nothing in accel/), then `IOAccelCommandQueue::init` and `attach(accelerator)` only; `IOAccelCommandQueue::connectClient` (an IOAccelSharedUserClient2 of the same accelerator and task) calls `start`, i.e. `IOAccelSubmitter2::start` (the 64-bit accumulatedGPUTime / lastSubmittedTime) and `commandQueueStart` (`IOAccelEventMachineFast2::initEvent` twice, the family's own, as `IOAccelContext2::contextStart` does for WindowServer's RD32DContext, and `IOAccelCommandQueueList::addCommandQueue`). Apple's Metal: `-[MTLIOAccelCommandQueue initWithDevice:descriptor:]` = `IOAccelCommandQueueCreateWithQoS([device sharedRef], qos)` (IOAccelerator.framework: IOServiceOpen(accel, type 8), IOConnectAddClient(queue, shared), then a block-fence notification port (selector 0) and the process path (selector 5)); Intel's MTLIGAccelCommandQueue subclasses it, AMD's Bronze driver uses it as is; `sharedRef` is set in `initWithAcceleratorPort:` (`supportLazyInitialization` is NO). The plugin (`metal/plugin/nvmtl_gpu_time_queue.h`, `tests/test_gpu_time_queue.c`; glue `nvmtl_gpu_time_queue_open` in NVMTLDevice.m, called at the end of `-[NVMTLDevice initWithAcceleratorPort:]`) opens one queue per process, the first two steps only (it submits nothing, so no notification port), with IOServiceOpen / IOConnectAddClient / IOServiceClose / IOAccelSharedGetConnect from dlsym (no IOKit link), keeps it until the process exits (one Metal device per process), closes it again when AddClient fails; knob `NVMTL_GPUTIME_QUEUE=0` switches it off (a line of rdna3mtl.conf; the environment only in a development build, a release build clears inherited NVMTL_ variables). Logs (once per process): `GPU time queue: IOAccelCommandQueue opened for <proc> (pid N; connection ...)`, `GPU time queue: FAILED at <step>, IOReturn 0x... for <proc> (pid N)`, `GPU time queue: off (NVMTL_GPUTIME_QUEUE=0) ...`. Kernel: the credit walk counts the IOAccelCommandQueues it sees (`debug.rdna3accel_perf_cmdqueues`; a queue is a direct child of the accelerator, `metaCast("IOAccelSubmitter2")` holds, its IOUserClientCreator is IOKit's `pid N, name`); the 'has no IOAccelSubmitter2' line names that count. Triage: `gpu_time_queue_rows` (the plugin lines: INFO opened, WARN per failure step and IOReturn, INFO off) and `gpu_util_verdict` (INFO perf_cmdqueues, WARN 0 queues while perf_nosubns > 0); `tests/test_triage_gpuq.py`. Risks: the start runs in WindowServer's device creation (a kernel path only contexts took before; a failure only logs); one more family user client per Metal process on the accelerator (the family's per-pid limit counts contexts + queues + shared + devices). The card must show: `perf_cmdqueues` about the number of Metal processes, `perf_nosubns` near 0, Chess / Terminal / Activity Monitor with % GPU while they draw, each process's `GPU time queue: ... opened` line, no FAILED line. ECC review round 1 (silent-failure-hunter, cpp-reviewer), fixed: the queue opens right after the allow-list check, before the device's first GPU work (hardware pools, gpupass probe, Vulkan), so the start-up gap is gone; every `GPU time queue:` line passes the release syslog filter (`nvmtl_log_failure`) and collect.sh's 24-log-coredisplay.txt / 26-log-metal-plugin.txt predicates also take the release prefix `NullMoth:`; the NULL-sharedRef line says `cannot`; a connection a failure left is closed and its IOServiceClose answer logged (`(the queue was closed again)` only on 0, else `(closing it FAILED too: IOReturn ...)`, triage WARN); NO_SHARED / OPEN_FAILED get one more try at the process's next Metal device (logged); the kernel counts a pid whose only submitter is not started yet (no accumulatedGPUTime: between IOServiceOpen and IOConnectAddClient) apart from a lasting creditfail (`perf_creditwait`, its time `perf_pendingns`, no 'has no IOAccelSubmitter2' line for it; the creditfail line names the class, as before); triage takes the pids of the kernel's no-submitter lines, subtracts the ones that logged `opened` (INFO start-up gap) and WARNs by name for a pid with a plugin device and no opened queue (with its failure step), WARNs for plugin device lines with no `GPU time queue:` line at all (an older plugin), and the 0-queues-at-the-last-walk row is INFO (the processes may have exited); `perf_creditwait` INFO up to 16, WARN above. Selector 5 (`s_set_get_info`, read from the family) only copies the process path into the submitter (+0xd8, read by `reportGPURestart` for the family's GPU-restart report), sets the QoS (already the default 2) and returns the trace ID: no registry property, nothing Activity Monitor or our report reads, so it stays out. ECC review round 2, fixed: a pid whose first try FAILED and whose retry opened is an INFO ('needed the retry'), not a WARN (a failed close stays a WARN: the unstarted queue remains); a device refused after the early open (Vulkan did not come up) closes its queue again (`GPU time queue: closed again for ...`, IOServiceClose logged) and lets the next device try; the start-up gap is worded as lost time (only time after the open is credited; the unstarted span is `perf_pendingns`, which also silences 'no process got GPU time'); plugin devices refused by the vendor check, the allow-list or Vulkan are not Metal devices in triage; the WindowServer log (32) and the ladder's log slices also take the release prefix `NullMoth:`. Limit: a release plugin does not syslog its initWithAcceleratorPort / refusal lines, so there a pid without a queue line reads as 'no Metal device' (INFO) unless it logged FAILED. (2) The video engines (VCN / JPEG) and the kernel's own SDMA copies are not counted; user-queue (MES) jobs are invisible to the kernel. (3) The SDMA fence raises no interrupt: its jobs end at a waiter's read or the next 250 ms take (busy time up to 250 ms long per idle gap). (4) `OSNumber::addValue` on the family's number is not atomic against the family's own add (only from block fences, never used here). (5) The card must show: Activity Monitor's GPU History moving with load and near 0 at idle, WindowServer with % GPU while windows move, `perf_rings` > 0, `perf_smu` 0. ECC review round 1 (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), fixed: the job recorded after the doorbell is read again at once (an EOP interrupt in between found nothing to end); every reset that forces fences (`forceFences`, the SDMA soft reset, the MES ring reset, `gpuReset`) ends the rings' jobs with their time (`rd3_busy_end_all`) before fence_seq starts again at 0; a process's time per take is capped at the wall time since the last take, one span carried, the rest counted (`perf_cappedns`; GFX + SDMA at once read above 100 %); the clock is read under the busy lock; fBusy goes out under its lock after the interrupt teardown; the take is answered before the DRM gate (NotReady without a GPU, not 'an older core'), ABI version 2; submitters found recursively in the accelerator's subtree (depth 4, child-list snapshots), 64-bit numbers checked, `lastSubmittedTime` problems counted; work the busy time misses counted per kind (`perf_unc_vcn` / `_jpeg` / `_other` / `_kcopy` / `_userq`, the first of each logged); the core's other totals exported (`perf_stale` / `_merged` / `_lostjobs` / `_inflight` / `_ringns_{gfx,comp,sdma}`), SDMA job ends per fence reader (`perf_sdmaend_{irq,wait,take,sub,reset}`), `perf_lastret` (logged on every change), `perf_winreset`, `perf_submitters`, `perf_uccbad`, `perf_nolast`; per-kind log budgets with the ring in the flush line; triage reads 13-ioreg-accel.txt against the counters and WARNs on stale flushes, uncounted work with no counted job and unreadable creators. Rejected: pinning fBringup without fDrmLock (platformPageoff pins it with fDrmLock too). ECC review round 2, fixed: `RDNA3Accel::stop` takes fBringup out under fDrmLock before stopAndFree (the take, VRAM copies, GPU VA calls and statsTick see nullptr instead of an object being freed; the copies and the fence wait re-check it under the lock); a fence waiter still inside the bring-up when the kext stops with DRM files open is not covered (as for the rings); a take charges each ring's running job up to now (amdgpu_ctx_fence_time's unfinished fence), so the wall-time cap drops only real overlap (a 2 s job on one ring kept about a quarter before); the extra fence read after a submission (the slot is in VRAM: an uncached BAR read) only when an interrupt landed since the doorbell; triage's 'every accumulatedGPUTime is 0' is INFO (the processes may have exited). ECC review round 3, fixed: `drmUserqWait` re-reads fBringup under the lock on every round of its wait (it read it once and would dereference the null stop() now sets). Open LOW: from stop()'s null until `teardownInterrupt`, `irqAction` drops interrupts (a stop during the MSI self-test reads as 'no SDMA trap interrupt', `MSIVerified=0`); `systemWillShutdown` and `RDNA3Resume.cpp` check fBringup unlocked (after PMstop / at shutdown only). | kext, accel, hw, common, metal, tests, tools, docs | M | done (host tests); card run open; (1) done in the plugin, card run open |

Static (restored) text is drawn right, live text is not. Open: the cause (outside what PARANOID changes: the translator / RADV build, the framebuffer-fetch blend over a LOAD pass of the IOSurface, or the per-frame managed upload range (a byte-exact copy of a range the app declared smaller than what it wrote; Apple copies whole pages), see docs/AFTER-TESTS.md); the Terminal quit by System Events does not work (`quit: still running after 8s, TERM` in every rung: Automation not answered), so the plugin's exit SUMMARY is lost and each rung waits 8 s GLYPH HUNT (quick/ladder2, host tests `tests/test_surf_census.c`, `tests/test_ab_switch.c`, `tests/test_ladder_step.sh`, `tests/test_triage_glyphs.py`, `tests/test_glyph_verdict.py`; probe `tests/vk_same_image_probe.c`; plugin NOT built on macOS this round (the Tahoe VM was off) and NOT seen on the card): (1) FOUND BY READING AND PROVEN ON THE DEV HOST: `nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_image_sub3` with ONE image on both sides moved it to TRANSFER_SRC and then to TRANSFER_DST and declared TRANSFER_SRC for the source, and an overlapping region (a scroll) is undefined in Vulkan; the probe on the RX 7800 XT (RADV 26.2.5, DCC on and off) shows disjoint copies right with the old layouts, but an overlapping copy in place (old layouts or GENERAL) leaves wrong regions in several readbacks of every run, and the new sequence (both sides GENERAL when the regions share no texel, a scratch image `image -> scratch -> image` when they do; scratch images live on `nvk_cmdbuf.tmpimg` until the command buffer retires; 3D, depth, multisample and compressed shapes still run in place and are counted `same_image_unsafe`) never; counters `same_image_copies/overlap/general/tmp/tmpfail/unsafe` in `vk: ab: SUMMARY`, the first 8 logged (`vk: same-image copy ...`), triage row `same-image copy`; buffer<->image paths checked: one image per call, layouts consistent, no change. If Terminal scrolls by copying its window onto itself (the `same-image copy` counter in its log says whether anything does), scrolled text was a candidate; it does not explain the progress Terminal (`tail -f`, not yet scrolling) or card runs 25..25c, so the glyph cause stays OPEN. (2) `NVMTL_MANAGED_WHOLE=1` / `NVMTL_MANAGED_PAGES=1` (`nvmtl_managed_widen`, logged at the first flush, in `vk: ab switches`; `managed: SUMMARY` gained `whole pages decl_bytes up_bytes widened_uploads`; triage WARN row `managed upload`). (3) The undeclared-write detector under `NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK=1`: a mirror per managed buffer (zero at creation, up to 8 MiB each and 64 MiB in all) of the shadow as the last scan saw it; every flush compares the shadow OUTSIDE the declared range, every submit scans the registered buffers that have no range (`nvmtl_undeclared_scan`, host-tested); hits are counted once, a hit declared at the next flush is `ud_late_bytes` (a race), buffers the GPU writes back are left out (`ud_gpu_off`); `managed: SUMMARY` gained `ud_*`, the first 4 hits per buffer and 64 per process are logged as `managed: UNDECLARED CPU write ...` with offsets and the distance to the declared range, triage row `undeclared writes`. The 25f managed rung (changed_exact=0) said nothing about writes OUTSIDE the declared range; this does. (4) The surface census (`metal/plugin/nvmtl_surf_census.h`, `census: SUMMARY` every 10 s while a counter moved and at exit, any process, no trace needed): per IOSurface the surface-in uploads (family VRAM / private / skipped / failed), render passes (cleared), draws (`buffer_image_blit_*`, framebuffer fetch), blit-encoder copies, replaceRegion, compute writes, presents, GPU writes published (dirty mark / copy-out); a page-off is kernel-side, what a process sees of it is a surface-in upload into a VRAM surface (the CPU copy was newer than the tag) and the dirty marks; triage row `surface census`. (5) `rdna3vm step ladder-glyphs` (`rdna3ladder=3`, `--ladder-glyphs`; replaces `ladder-paranoid`): baseline, managed-whole, managed-check, no-surface-share, no-surface-pageoff, fbfetch-zero, managed-pages, managed-eager-zero (=1 is the old behaviour), no-texbuf, no-bindless, dontcare-clears, skip-empty-0, paranoid (negative control); a second hi-res look 3 s after the first and `glyph_verdict.verdict_files(later=...)` counts only the tick-line window that CHANGED between them (a restored or frozen window is `STATIC ... ignored`, none live = `none`); triage rows `ladder glyphs <rung>` (what a clean rung means), `ladder glyphs control`, `ladder glyphs none`. (6) The quit needs no consent: SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGKILL with a wait after each, `quit: pid N ended by SIGINT after Ns` per instance, triage row `ladder quit` (a signal skips the plugin's exit SUMMARY; its 10 s lines are the evidence). (7) `nvlog` lines are cut at 1023 characters now (767 before: the long SUMMARY lines were truncated). About the early fences of 25f (WindowServer 5 in 91994 witnessed submits, worst 867 us, Chess once) no rung switches the kernel side: `sync` (`NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0`) of `ladder` is the nearest plugin-side check, and `fence early` per process is the number to watch; a fence that completes before the GPU's writes are visible would hit a CPU readback right after it (a page-off copy), a kext/hw question left to the manager. ECC ROUND 1 (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter) of the glyph hunt, fixed: a rung is judged only when its Terminal pid logged in the slice (baseline too) and only when its knob was ENGAGED (`KNOB NOT ENGAGED` rows from the effective-state lines and counters: `widened_uploads`, `ud_whole_marks`, `zr_forced`, new `dontcare_seen/dontcare_cleared`, the `item 7` / page-off / zero-fill / texbuf / bindless / skip-empty lines); one shared 10 s timer for the managed / census / ab / fbfetch summaries (signals skip atexit); the detector watches every buffer whose `contents` was read (`ud_managed` counts the rest), `NVMTL_MANAGED_WHOLE` also uploads watched buffers the app wrote without a didModifyRange (`ud_whole_marks`), `NVMTL_MANAGED_CHECK` perturbs timing (documented); no glyph verdict is silent (a rung with a hires look and no verdict, or a report that is not a .tar.gz, is `none` with the reason; `push: N ppm file(s) not sent`, `ABORTED by SIGx at rung N`, look `exit:` codes, a truncated log slice, tick windows of an earlier run); the census counts lock-free (slot found once per pass, blit flag once per pipeline state); the surfin SUMMARY is two lines; the same-image scratch image is reused per command buffer; glyph_verdict counts a stack's change only in its own columns and four pitches below, and lists the pipes judged | plugin, tests, tools, docs | M | done (diagnostics, host tests, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode); card run 2026-10-25f says PARANOID does not cure it, cause open |
| N51 | Surface-in cost (card run 2026-10-25e, WindowServer: surface-in checks 38 % of the encode time, 104 us per call, windows get slower with more elements). MEASURED from the report (tools/plugin_trace.py data joined by command buffer) and on the Tahoe VM (`IOSurfaceGetSeed` 0.003 us, the VRAM tag query 0.8 us, the pending-write scan 1.3 us, the surface monitor 0.06 us, an upload's lock/unlock 1.9 us): of the 1607 ms, 466 surface UPLOADs are 399 ms (25 %, 855 us each: window surfaces of 971x680 that their producer rewrites per frame, seed moved), the cheap seed checks about 12 ms (1.6 to 5.5 us per call), and ~1100 ms (75 %) are calls of 30 to 300 us in command buffers WITHOUT any SURF_IN record: buffer textures the GPU cannot alias (`buffer texture: ... copy (pitch)`, 112 of the first 128, bytesPerRow not a multiple of 256) are private images refreshed from the buffer's CPU bytes once per command buffer, each refresh a synchronous submit + wait (nvmtl_vk_image_write, ~80 us even for a few KB) that the trace never recorded (the earlier reading 'a lock, a seed query and a tag check per call' was wrong: the decision is not the cost). The same path is the image churn: copy textures were never parked (`_bparkable = !copied`), so each new one made and later destroyed an image. DONE (plugin, host test `tests/test_surfin_cache.c`, `metal/plugin/nvmtl_surfin_cache.h`, built on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode, NOT seen on the card; kill switch `NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1`, also in the PARANOID profile (15 switches), logged `vk: surfin cache ON/OFF` and in `vk: ab switches`): (1) a lock-free probe answers a surface texture whose seed (and tag generation for a private image) did not move before the pending-write scan and the monitor (the decision is the same as the locked path's first test, which still decides every miss); (2) a buffer texture's refresh compares its source with a shadow of the bytes it last uploaded and skips an equal one (shadow = the exact bytes the upload reads, valid only after a successful upload, dropped by nvmtl_surf_dirty (every blit / mipmap / resolve write) and both replaceRegion variants; up to 1 MiB per image, 32 MiB per process, padding bytes ignored); (3) a pool of 16 private copy images (4 MiB) parked by shape at dealloc and reused by the next copy texture (the new texture's first refresh always uploads); (4) `vk: surfin: SUMMARY` (nvlog_uncapped, <= every 10 s, at exit: calls, fast, misses by reason, locked uploads / skips, refreshes uploaded / skipped / without shadow / failed / forgotten with their us, shadow bytes, copy images new / reused / parked / evicted), triage row `surface-in cache`, and ENC_SPLIT words 0..7 (buffer-texture refresh uploads / skips with their us, probe hits, monitor wait, surface uploads; `tools/plugin_trace.py`). Expected: the ~1100 ms mostly gone for textures whose bytes do not change between frames (an upload of a few KB is ~80 us, an unchanged compare 1 to 3 us), the probe saves 1 to 2 us on each of the 15 K calls; the 399 ms of real surface uploads is NOT cut. Open: (a) the card run (compare `bufcopy_skipped` / `bufcopy_uploaded` and the `ENC_SPLIT` extras with 2026-10-25e; `NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1` first if an artefact appears); (b) the surface uploads themselves (no dirty range exists, only the seed; a producer's per-frame copy-out is a full upload): record small uploads into the command buffer instead of a synchronous submit + wait, or share the producer's VRAM (the 89 refused kernel imports, EPERM, are why those surfaces are private); (c) no ladder rung for the switch (the PARANOID rung covers it); (d) the per-draw cost outside surface-in (60 % of the encoder time is the app's own, not split)  ECC round 1 (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter) fixed: replaceRegion forgets the shadow AFTER the image write (both variants; host test), a refresh is skipped only when the image rests in SHADER_READ_ONLY (`bufcopy_skip_refused_layout`), the SUMMARY comes from a 10 s timer with seq / t_ms (WindowServer never exits cleanly) and carries sin_us_total, fast_us, the refresh times, the `calls` breakdown, noshadow us, forgotten, refusals by cause, `cimg_on`, `NVMTL_SURFIN_VERIFY=N` (every Nth skip reads the image back and compares: the hard stale-image number, FAIL in triage), triage prints all of it and WARNs for a WindowServer without a SUMMARY. N50 side finding: render encoders record no layout transition for a sampled texture (a render target rests in TRANSFER_SRC, a compute-written one in GENERAL, the descriptor declares SHADER_READ_ONLY); counted per layout (`rsample_by_layout`, triage row `sampled layout`); `tests/vk_meta_probe.c` LIE=1/2 on the dev host's RADV 26.2.5 (DCC on and off): no wrong pixel, so no transition was added (it would need the pass ended and restarted per texture); if the card's Mesa differs, the count says how often it happens. | plugin, tests, tools, docs | S | done (host test, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode); card run open |
| N52 | Chess artefacts in EVERY rung of the 2026-10-25f A/B ladder (cyan streaks on the pieces, small red bits; PARANOID included, so not scheduling or reuse). ROOT CAUSE, certain from the plugin logs of all 8 Chess processes (the 7 ladder rungs and the main log; `reports/2026-10-25f`, 26-log-metal-plugin.txt and 41-ladder-*.log, 8 to 16 times per ~70 s per process): Apple's GL resolves the STENCIL of its 4-sample D32_S8 framebuffer (glBlitFramebuffer; Metal's blit has no stencil resolve, the depth goes through the `texturedFragment2DDepthResolve` draw) in five steps, all visible in the log: (1) blit 4-sample stencil -> buffer (`copy_image_to_buffer`, vk format 130, aspect option 4 = stencil, bytesPerRow 3884 = 971 * 4 samples * 1 B, buffer offset 0, a 4 MiB buffer); (2) blit the buffer -> a NEW 4-sample R8_UINT texture (`image_create: 971x680 4-sample colour target`, `copy_buffer_to_image`, vk format 13, the same row 3884); (3) a render pass with a RESOLVE attachment 4-sample R8_UINT -> 1-sample R8_UINT (`begin_render: par1 4-sample pass resolves colour 0x0 of 0x1`; an integer resolve takes sample 0); (4) blit the 1-sample R8_UINT -> buffer (row 971, `copy_image_to_buffer: recording vk format 13`); (5) blit the buffer -> the 1-sample D32_S8 stencil (`copy_buffer_to_image: recording (depth/stencil, ...) vk format 130 ... aspect 4 element 1 B`). The plugin DROPPED (1) (`REFUSED - the image is multisampled`, kept refused by N48 on the guess that Metal's blit cannot read a multisample texture - wrong: Apple's GL does it for the stencil) and (2) (`REFUSED - a 4-sample image: this colour format is not carried`, R8_UINT was outside the N48 upload pass), so (3)-(5) copied an UNINITIALISED 4-sample texture into the 1-sample stencil once per resolve; the board reflections are stencil-masked, hence garbage streaks that no scheduling switch can change. What Apple's blit does with a multisample texture (found from the numbers, the only evidence there is): the buffer row is `width * samples * bytes` (3884), i.e. element x * samples + s is sample s of pixel x; nothing in the log says more, and the two copies of the chain only have to agree with each other. Done (plugin `metal/plugin`, host tests `tests/test_msdl.c` + `tests/test_msupload.c`, probes `tests/vk_msdl_probe.c` + `tests/vk_msupload_probe.c`, triage rows in `tools/triage_n48.py`): (a) download pass, `nvmtl_msdl.h` (decision, pure C99) + `nvmtl_msbuf.h` (the layout and the word walk) + `nvmtl_msdl_spv.h` (glslc, spirv-val clean): `copy_image_to_buffer` of a 2/4/8-sample 2D texture runs a compute shader (`nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_msimage_to_buffer`) that reads the image through a SAMPLED view of the aspect (texelFetch per sample: stencil through a usampler2DMS view of the stencil aspect, depth through texture2DMS, colour through the UNORM twin of an sRGB image) and writes the buffer by device address, one invocation per aligned 32-bit word (a word that is only partly inside the region is merged with two atomics, so the bytes around the region, an unaligned offset and the row padding are never touched); carried: stencil of S8/D16_S8/D24_S8/D32_S8, depth of D16/D32/D16_S8/D32_S8 (24-bit depth refused, no byte layout in Metal), colour RGBA8/BGRA8/R8/RG8 UNORM and sRGB, R8/RG8/RGBA8/R16/R32 UINT; every 4-sample D/S image already has SAMPLED usage (`NVMTL_DEPTH_USAGE`), colour MS images too, so no image-creation change was needed (a missing usage is refused with a line); (b) upload pass extended (`nvmtl_msupload.h`): R8/RG8/RGBA8/R16/R32 UINT through a second fragment module with an unsigned output (R8_UINT is Chess's), and Apple's layout: a row of at least `width * samples * bytes` is sample-interleaved, one draw per sample restricted by the shader's SampleMask (a shorter row keeps the old one-element-per-pixel semantics); (c) logs and counters (no sysctl: per-process rate-limited lines with running counts, like `msupload:`): `msdl: N-sample image WxH -> buffer (vk format F, aspect A via view V, mode M): region ... row R B, K word(s) in GXxGY group(s) [S so far]` (first 8, then every 1024th), `msdl: pass built for the ... module`, `msdl: descriptor pool N opened`, `msdl: FAILED - ... [F failed, R refused, D done so far]`, `msdl: REFUSED - ...`, `copy_image_to_buffer: REFUSED - a N-sample image: <reason> (...)`, the G14 census line now says `multisample, carried by the plugin's download pass (view format V, mode M)`, and `msupload:` lines name the layout (`sample-interleaved` / `one element per pixel`) and the output (`integer` / `float`); triage rows `multisample download` (INFO when it ran, WARN for `msdl: FAILED`) and `plugin copy refused` (also for `msdl: REFUSED`), test `tests/test_triage_n48.py`; docs/AFTER-TESTS.md has the lines and the Chess stencil-resolve paragraph. Probe on the dev host's RX 7800 XT (RADV Mesa 26.2.5; the plugin's Mesa d381c361 was not compared): `tests/vk_msdl_probe.c` fills 2/4/8-sample D32_S8 and D16 images with a different stencil and depth value per (10x10 tile, sample) (draws with a static sample mask, depth from the viewport range, stencil by REPLACE with a dynamic reference), downloads whole images and sub-regions at unaligned offsets with padded rows and 0xAA guard bytes: every byte equal, nothing outside touched (320 draws, 971x680 whole image in 0.6 ms); colour roundtrip upload -> download for RGBA8, BGRA8, both sRGB, R8, RG8, R8_UINT, RG8_UINT, RGBA8_UINT, R16_UINT, R32_UINT at 2/4/8 samples with unaligned source/destination offsets: every sample equal; and Chess's whole chain at 971x680 (stencil -> buffer row 3884 -> 4-sample R8_UINT -> vkCmdResolveImage -> 1-sample R8_UINT -> buffer row 971): all 660280 pixels equal sample 0 of the stencil. Negative control (expected samples permuted) fails 178500 bytes, so the probe bites. `tests/vk_msupload_probe.c` (push constants and the integer module changed) 13 cases 0 wrong. The plugin builds on the macOS 26.4.1 VM (Xcode), 333 warnings (unchanged, none in this code). Other Chess findings (same logs): nothing else wrong - `reflection: ... has no AIR`, `no AIR` for the GL helper shaders and the `fixIndexBuffer` / `dsTextureToBuffer` compute kernels (SPIR-V cache HITs) are normal; `dsTextureToBuffer` reads stencil views (`newTextureView: depth/stencil view format 261 aspect 4`) right after step 5 (inferred: of the 1-sample stencil), so it too depends on this fix; the framebuffer fetch never fires in Chess (no `vk: fbfetch:` line), msupload had never run on the card before. Open / risks: (1) the order of elements inside a pixel's samples is inferred (sample index fast); it only matters if Apple reads the buffer with the CPU or another shader between the copies, and the log would show that as a `copy_image_to_buffer: ... row` for the same buffer (it does not in Chess); an integer resolve is sample 0, so the result is the stencil's sample 0 whatever the order; (2) Apple's blit may have meant a resolve (average) for colour/depth - the plugin's copy keeps every sample, which is the only reading that fits a buffer of `samples` elements per pixel; (3) a compute pass per resolve costs ~0.2 ms for 971x680 on the dev host, cheap; the first use per process builds two pipelines (logged); (4) D24_S8 depth, multisample arrays, RGBA16F/10-bit colour and a multisample depth/stencil UPLOAD stay refused with a line; (5) the sampled view of a depth/stencil image leaves it in SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL (the existing copy/render paths transition from the tracked layout, as after any sampled use). ECC review round 1 (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter), all findings fixed: (F1) every msdl/msupload event is counted and the totals go out as `msdl: SUMMARY` / `msupload: SUMMARY` (nvlog_uncapped, key=value; first within ~10 s, then every 10 s while moving, at a power of two of an error counter, at exit; `nvmtl_msstat.h`, host test in `tests/test_msdl.c`; both lines pass the release syslog filter), the success lines have a literal per aspect (STENCIL / DEPTH / COLOUR) and per upload layout; (F2) triage row `multisample chain` (stencil downloads, R8_UINT uploads, resolves into R8_UINT per process; WARN when a link is 0, they differ by more than 25 %, or there is no SUMMARY) with `resolve_r8u` counted in `nvmtl_vk_cmd_begin_pass` for every multisample pass (also the empty resolve pass) and a first-per-format `begin_render: par1 first resolve into a format-F ...` line; (F3) the flat-layout fallback is counted (`flat`, `up_flat_F`), has its own `msupload: FLAT layout fallback (why)` line and a WARN row; (F4) a literal per refusal / failure kind, the distinct reason of a plan refusal logged at its 1st, 2nd, 4th ... occurrence, per-kind totals in the SUMMARY; (F5) the census says `format/aspect carried` (the region is decided at the copy); (F6/F7) triage strips the counters from reasons, keeps the parentheses inside them, shows 8, and takes totals from the SUMMARY; (F8) no-device and no-address are separate kinds, the pool-full message carries pools and sets, no meaningless `(0)` codes, `blit copyFrom...: FAILED` lines carry the reason (`nvmtl_vk_copy_reason`); (cpp 1) the download puts the source image back into the layout it had (a following LOAD pass trusts the tracked layout); (cpp 2) interleaved only when `row % (samples * bytes) == 0` and the row holds a full row of the region, a row more than 255 B beyond that is flagged WIDE (log, SUMMARY `wide`, WARN row); (cpp 3) the last 32-bit word may end beyond the Metal length when the buffer is a plain allocation (not pooled / placed / imported / sparse / BAR1) whose allocation covers it. Probes re-run on the RX 7800 XT: all equal. Card run open: Chess must show `msdl: SUMMARY d_stencil=N`, `msupload: SUMMARY up_inter_13=N`, `resolve_r8u=N` about equal (triage row `multisample chain` INFO), no `plugin copy refused` for vk format 130/13, and no streaks | plugin, tests, tools, docs | M | done (host tests, probes on RADV, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode); card run open |

Why PR #215: master's `thread_invoke`/`thread_dispatch` non-monotonic-time patch does not match Darwin 25.4.0+ (macOS 26.4, the owner's 26.4.1); the PR adds the matching one (reported working on 26.4.1 and on 26.7.1). The image's OpenCore is 1.0.6 (RELEASE, sha256 match with the acidanthera zip), which has `ProvideCurrentCpuInfo` for AMD families and the KVM `-cpu host` MSR 35h fix; `ocvalidate` 1.0.6 passes on the edited config and the switched-back one. The run prints `VM CPU: ...` / `amd-cpu: on, ...`, `last-cpu` becomes `vm-cpu:` in `00-host.txt`, `rdna3vm check` has a "VM CPU" part (host clocksource tsc, invariant TSC). **First card-VM boot 2026-10-26 (8 cores, serial-window-20261026-002857.log): the kernel booted with all patches in place, then panicked during IOKit matching (`Kernel trap type 14`, `CR2 = RIP = _copypv+0x1cc`, error code 3). Cause (code analysis of the owner's Darwin 25.4.0 kernel and Boot KC with `tools/vm/ocpatchsim.py`; none of the 24 patches touches the site): the `VMCALL` of macOS 26.4's paravirtual ticket-lock kick (enabled in `i386_init` when KVM CPUID bit `kvm-pv-unhalt` is set and boot-arg `pvticket` is not 0), which KVM on an AMD host rewrites to `VMMCALL` in the read-only guest text (`emulator_fix_hypercall`), so the write faults. `-cpu host` exposes the bit, the old Skylake-Client model did not. Fix: `-kvm-pv-unhalt` in `CPU_HOST_BASE` (`pvticket=0` in `BASE_ARGS` is the second switch); needs the VM to confirm. A 12-vCPU try hung after `EXITBS:START`, before the kernel ran: not explained by the patches, use `CPUS=8`. One entry never applies: AMD_Vanilla's `Force CPUFAMILY_INTEL_PENRYN 11.3+` has Base `_cpuid_set_info ` with a trailing space (same on bare metal).** **Open (needs the VM/card):** that macOS 26.4.1 boots in KVM with these patches and `-kvm-pv-unhalt`; the TSC frequency source under `vmware-cpuid-freq` with an AMD guest vendor; PAT patch choice (Algrey's default; Shaneee's maps GPU memory differently, may matter for our driver's CPU mappings); the IOPCIFamily patches are not applied in the VM (the bare-metal EFI carries the "Disable 10 bit tags" ones, `docs/BAREMETAL.md` part 6); a later Darwin needs a newer `patches.plist` (look at the AMD_Vanilla PR list first); parity with the bare-metal boot (`package/oc_install.sh` does not touch CPU patches); the OSX-KVM `config.plist` in the image has a malformed `DeviceProperties/Add` (a `<dict>` missing: `ocvalidate` stops at the serialisation errors before checking anything else, so validate a repaired copy) | tools, tests, docs | M  Verified 2026-10-26: macOS 26.4.1 booted to the desktop in `rdna3vm window` with `-cpu host,kvm=on,-kvm-pv-unhalt,+invtsc,vmware-cpuid-freq=on,check -smp 8,sockets=1,cores=8,threads=1` (AMD_Vanilla core count 8); About This Mac names the SMBIOS model's CPU ("8-Core Intel Xeon W"), as on AMD hackintoshes. **CPU name (owner's request 2026-10-26, host tests only):** `rdna3vm`'s OpenCore step (`cmd__oc`, `oc_cpu_kexts`) now also injects Acidanthera's RestrictEvents 1.1.6 (CPU brand string patch, on by default for a non-Intel CPU, name from CPUID, no boot-arg) with Lilu 1.7.2 (the image's Lilu 1.6.8 is inert on Darwin 25; its other plugins, WhateverGreen 1.6.7 and VirtualSMC 1.3.3, end at Sequoia by their own range, so Lilu 1.7.2 wakes none of them; the old Lilu is kept as `Lilu.kext.rdna3vm-orig` and back when the mode is off). The RELEASE zips are fetched once into `~/rdna3-vm/oc-kexts` with pinned sha256 (`tools/vm/data/oc_kexts.SOURCE`); a failed fetch is a loud `!! RestrictEvents is not injected` line and the VM boots anyway. AUDIT against amdgpu 6.12 (default profile, `rdna3amdgpu=0`; certain = from code): (a) `gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_fence` / user fence, `gfx_v11_0_emit_mem_sync` (ACQUIRE_MEM GCR GL2/GLM WB+INV, GL1/GLV/GLK/GLI INV), `gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_ib_gfx/compute`, `emit_hdp_flush` (CP0<<pipe from the PFP, CP2<<pipe on MEC), `emit_vm_flush` (+PFP_SYNC_ME), `emit_cntxcntl`: identical packets (certain); ours emits the mem sync at every job where amdgpu needs AMDGPU_IB_FLAG_EMIT_MEM_SYNC (stronger). (b) `amdgpu_vm_flush`'s pipeline sync before a VM flush and `amdgpu_ib_schedule`'s pipeline sync for an explicit / same-ring implicit dependency (`amdgpu_cs_sync_rings` -> `explicit_sync`): the default path never emits it (certain); the CS ioctl waits for SYNCOBJ_IN / DEPENDENCIES on the CPU instead (covers explicit deps), and VMIDs are per file (no VMID reuse under a running job), but there is NO implicit sync for a surface two processes share: a reader's job may overlap the writer's on the ring unless userspace waited for the writer's fence (inferred: matters only without synchronous commits) -> switch pipe_sync. (c) The fence's place: amdgpu keeps it in `adev->wb` (GTT, system memory; `amdgpu_fence_driver_start_ring`), ours in a VRAM wb slot read through the BAR (certain). The witness's data are in GTT (memory type 2): with the fence in VRAM the CPU can see the fence (a local VRAM write, read by a BAR read) before the job's GL2 write-back to system memory (posted PCIe writes) is visible; in amdgpu both go the same path (inferred mechanism of "fence early", rare and short as observed). (d) `sdma_v6_0_ring_emit_fence` (FENCE MTYPE UC + TRAP), `emit_hdp_flush`, `emit_mem_sync` (GCR_REQ): same packets; the default DMA path has no in-ring HDP / VM flush (the commit's MMIO HDP flush and vmMap's MMIO TLB flush of both hubs cover them; certain). (e) PTEs: `gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte`'s MTYPE_UC override for COHERENT / UNCACHED / EXT_COHERENT BOs was missing (certain; RADV also asks UC in the VA flags for those, so no change in practice); `gmc_v11_0_map_mtype` same; system pages are always SNOOPED (amdgpu: only cached TT; ours is the stronger, coherent choice); every user BO NC by default as amdgpu. Two VMIDs map an IOSurface's VRAM both NC (GL2 is physically tagged, the next job's GL2_INV covers CPU writes; no MTYPE conflict found). (f) HDP 6.0 has no read cache (`hdp_v6_0_funcs` has no invalidate_hdp, no HDP_READ_CACHE_INVALIDATE register): nothing to invalidate around CPU BAR reads (certain). (g) **BUG (certain, fixed)**: `hw/xfer.c` (the IOSurface page-off and evict / restore through the 16 MiB GART window) flushed only the MMHUB TLB, but SDMA 6.0 translates through the GFXHUB (`sdma_v6_0_sw_init`: `ring->vm_hub = AMDGPU_GFXHUB(0)`; `amdgpu_gart_invalidate_tlb` flushes every hub): the second chunk of a copy larger than the window, or a copy after another one with other pages, went to / came from the previous pages; the staged page-off of card run 26b reuses one contiguous staging buffer, which hid it there (a new staging buffer, a per-page page-off or an evict after it would not be). (h) Freed BOs: amdgpu keeps a BO's memory until its fences signal (TTM delayed delete) and clears unmapped PTEs only after the VM's jobs (`amdgpu_vm_clear_freed` syncs on the root resv); ours frees and unmaps at once (certain; harmless while userspace waits, as the plugin's PARANOID profile does; open). DONE: `hw/gmc11.c` `gmc11_gart_tlb_flush` (both hubs) used by `xfer()` (sim: the GFXHUB VMID0 TLB is modelled for SDMA GART accesses, `S.sdma_tlb_stale`; the old code fails the new checks, a second window of other pages and the way back); `rdna3_gfx_ring` / `rdna3_sdma_ring` `fence_sys_mc` / `fence_sys_cpu`: every fence of the GFX, compute and SDMA rings is ALSO written to a system page bound in the GART under the user-queue slots (`RDNA3Bringup::cohSetup` at the end of hwInit, again after a reset / wake; self-test per ring, a copy that does not land is switched off and logged), the same RELEASE_MEM / FENCE packet, before the VRAM one, no interrupt (sim asserts both packets); each fence check reads the VRAM value then the copy (`common/rdna3_coh.h` `rd3_coh_fence_check`, `tests/test_coh.c`) and counts a VRAM value ahead of its copy (`skews`, their length, max): the measured form of "fence early"; `gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte`'s UC override ported (`cohPte`, counted `uc_bo`). A/B SWITCHES (runtime sysctl, default 0 = before N54, boot-arg `rdna3coh=<mask>`, each change logged `coherence: ... switches now ...`, jobs counted per switch): `debug.rdna3_coh_fence_sys` (the copy answers, amdgpu's place; a waiter whose VRAM fence passed spins for the copy, a copy 50 ms behind goes STALE and the VRAM fence answers), `_fence_inv` (RELEASE_MEM + GL2_INV/GL1_INV/GLV_INV), `_pipe_sync` (`gfx11_emit_pipeline_sync` at every job, gfx and compute, also the exact path), `_wait_idle` (the CS ioctl waits for its own fence, 200 ms bound), `_uc` (new user mappings MTYPE_UC), `_hdp_end` (HDP flush after the IBs), `_vm_flush` (VM flush at every job), `_sdma_gcr` (kernel SDMA copies / fills / rects between GCR_REQ and an HDP flush; sim counts the packets), `_all`, `_mask`; `debug.rdna3_coh` the report; `tools/macos/collect.sh` `42-coherence.txt`; `tools/triage_coherence.py` rows `coherence switches`, `fence copy`, `fence skew` (WARN with skews, FAIL stale / self-test), `wait idle`, `uncached maps`, `sdma gcr`, `vm flush` (`tests/test_triage_coherence.py`); the ladder variant 4 (`autoreport.sh --ladder-coh`, `rdna3ladder=4`, `tools/ladder-steps.txt` `ladder-coh`): baseline, coh-all (positive control), then one switch per rung, set / read back / put back around the rung, triage `ladder-coh <rung>` + `ladder-coh verdict`. docs/AFTER-TESTS.md "Kernel coherence switches (N54)". | N55 | Terminal's broken glyphs after card run 2026-10-26c (text right up to about column 37, broken further right; every plugin A/B and every N54 kernel coherence switch keeps it). ROOT CAUSE NOT ESTABLISHED; a draw capture is in for the next card run. FOUND: (1) the text is Core Animation's glyph cache, not a 256-px property of the window (certain from QuartzCore 26.4.1, `CA::OGL::GlyphCache::emit_glyphs` / `flush`): each new glyph's A8 bitmap is appended to a pooled sub-allocation of a 256 KB managed MTLBuffer at the cache's fill offset, the new bytes are declared with didModifyRange (`MetalContext::mark_modified_buffer`: location = sub-allocation offset + flushed, length = fill - flushed), and every glyph is one instance of `buffer_image_blit_vert/frag_lpf` (TriangleStrip, 4 vertices, rect {min, size, offset = the glyph's cache offset, bpr = width} at vertex buffer 1, coverage at fragment buffer 9); the zoomed crop shows WHOLE OTHER GLYPHS at the right places ('o' drawn as 'r', 'vwxyz' as 'ecrg'), i.e. a rect read another glyph's coverage bytes (inferred), and the 'x threshold' is cache insertion order (glyphs first drawn further right sit later in the cache; inferred); lines with the same letters in the same columns differ, so it is the state when each line was drawn. RULED OUT: (a) the translation of both blit shaders (certain: the SPIR-V the card ran, taken from the Tahoe disk's spvcache, against the AIR: floor/fmin/offset+x+y*bpr, an 8-bit load, ushort vertex/instance ids widened, the 24-byte rect stride and member offsets 0/8/16/20; no 8/16-bit truncation, fp16, shift by 8, clamp or 256-wide anything); (b) the wrapper's lowering passes (certain from code: wide loads fuse only aligned u32 pairs, the SAFE align guard only lowers Aligned literals, physical bounds only drops InBounds); (c) byte-unaligned managed uploads (dynamic: a host probe on this RX 7800 XT / RADV 26.2.5, 5000 frames of glyph-like unaligned ranges plus 16-aligned rect ranges, split and as the plugin's per-buffer envelope, with the plugin's prologue barriers: byte exact); (d) undeclared or in-flight CPU writes (card run 2026-10-26b `managed-check`: changed_exact=0, one 178-byte race declared at the next flush); (e) the address table missing an offset (certain: entries are address + offset, setBufferOffset marks the set dirty, buffers are never descriptor-copied); (f) the render target pitch (608 px x 4 B rounded to 256 B is the IOSurface's 2560: RADV's own pitch would agree; inferred). NOT DONE: Apple's AMDRadeonX6000MTLDriver was not read; no Vulkan replay of the blit on a pitched linear target (the capture gives its exact inputs). ADDED (plugin, built on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode, 0 new warnings; host test `tests/test_capture.c` + `tools/capture_decode.py --check` in make test): `NVMTL_CAPTURE_DRAW=<shader>:<first>[:<count>[:<every>]][@<process>]` (off by default, draws nothing differently; `metal/plugin/nvmtl_capture.h`): the matching draws (function name substring, ordinal among matching draws) are written each to `/var/tmp/rdna3-nvmtl/capture-<process>-<pid>-<k>.bin` when their command buffer completes: SPIR-V, AIR (first time per function), constants as the fc cache's JSON key, draw arguments, target, viewport, scissor, the address table, and every bound buffer three times (CPU at the draw, CPU at completion, the GPU's VRAM copy read back at completion by a synchronous copy that skips the pre-submit hook, so no pending managed upload is flushed by the capture; the file is created through an O_NOFOLLOW directory fd with an owner check, a stale own file of an earlier boot replaced), caps 4 MiB per binding, 48 MiB per draw, 160 MiB per process, 32 draws, DROP records instead of silent cuts; log lines `capture: NVMTL_CAPTURE_DRAW=... ON/OFF/MALFORMED`, `capture: draw #N (...) taken`, `-> path: bytes, records, DROPPED`, `NOT written` / `NOT captured`. `tools/macos/collect_plugin_trace.sh` (so collect.sh) copies them to `33-plugin-capture/` with the trace checks (at most 64 files, 512 MiB, NVMTL_COLLECT_CAPTOTAL); `tools/capture_decode.py <report>` prints each draw, cpu_draw vs gpu_done per buffer and, for buffer_image_blit, every rect whose coverage or rect bytes differ, `--out DIR` writes the records and the coverage drawn from CPU and from GPU bytes as PGM. NEXT CARD RUN: rdna3mtl.conf `NVMTL_CAPTURE_DRAW=buffer_image_blit:60:24:11@Terminal`, the usual Terminal ladder baseline; then decode: differing GPU bytes = upload / coherence (and which: rect or coverage), identical bytes = replay the captured draw on the host's RADV. |

DONE: (1) Mesa patch `userspace/mesa/patches/0010-ac-attribute-ring-big-page-switch.patch`: `RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0` clears `discardable_allows_big_page` (stderr note; default upstream); the plugin routes `NVMTL_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0` to it like NVMTL_RADV_DEBUG (nvmtl_vk.c `nvmtl_radv_env`, put back after vkCreateDevice; `nvmtl_attr_ring_value` in nvmtl_ab_switch.h, host test tests/test_ab_switch.c; the knob is in `nvmtl_ab_knobs`, so `vk: ab switches` and the `plugin switch` row show it; log `vk: RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE="0" (NVMTL_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE="0") - the attribute ring's BIG_PAGE is OFF ...`). (3) Kernel logs: `GEM DISCARDABLE #n: <size> KiB, alignment <a> KiB, VRAM beyond BAR0|visible at 0x<off> (64 KiB aligned yes|no, 2 MiB aligned yes|no)` or `... GTT, VRAM was asked for and refused - WRONG for the attribute ring ...` (RDNA3Accel::discardableNote, first 16 then every 1024th, GTT always) and `GEM_VA DISCARDABLE #n: VA 0x.. + <size> KiB -> contiguous VRAM 0x.. (PA 0x..), PTE fragment F (<KiB>, 4 KiB PTEs, rdna3amdgpu=0 | amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment): BIG_PAGE ok | WRONG: <why>` (RDNA3Bringup::discardableMapNote; `gpuvm_map_fragment`, checked in sim_navi32; verdict from common/rdna3_attr_ring.h, host test tests/test_attr_ring.c). (4) Triage `attribute ring` (tools/triage_attr_ring.py, test tests/test_triage_attr_ring.py): PASS when every DISCARDABLE mapping is VRAM, 64 KiB aligned and congruent with fragment >= 4, FAIL otherwise (with the default kext it is FAIL: fragment 0), WARN without lines; `attr ring switch` names the processes RADV_ATTR_RING_BIG_PAGE=0 reached; `ladder attrring` WARNs when the plugin did not hand the knob to RADV, `ladder glyphs attrring` names the ring when that rung is clean under a broken baseline. A/B PLAN (next card run, `rdna3vm step ladder` after `rdna3vm update`, the Mesa bundle rebuilt by CI `full: true`): attrring CLEAN with baseline BROKEN = BIG_PAGE over our ring mapping is the cause; fix in the kernel, port, don't invent: map VRAM BOs with amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment fragments on the default path (as rdna3amdgpu=1 already does; `rdna3vm step amdgpu` with the default BIG_PAGE is the cross-check) and keep the ring in VRAM; then drop the rung. attrring STILL BROKEN = not the ring's BIG_PAGE: next replay a capture on the card's own RADV (N56: build tests/vk_capture_replay.c for macOS against the bundle's libvulkan_radeon) to split our Mesa build from the kernel. CARD RUN 2026-10-26h (reports/2026-10-26h, `rdna3vm step ladder`): the `attrring` rung (BIG_PAGE off) made Terminal's text and Chess fully clean (glyph verdict CLEAN 17/17, screenshots clean), the baseline of the same boot broken as always: BIG_PAGE over our ring mapping IS the cause. The kernel's lines (only in serial-20261026-184402.log; the triage row said "no `GEM DISCARDABLE` line" because it read only the archive's log): `GEM DISCARDABLE #10: 4224 KiB, alignment 2048 KiB, VRAM beyond BAR0 at 0x1a200000 (64 KiB aligned yes, 2 MiB aligned yes)`, `GEM_VA DISCARDABLE #10: VA 0x800000200000 + 4224 KiB -> contiguous VRAM 0x1a200000 (PA 0x1a200000), PTE fragment 0 (4 KiB, 4 KiB PTEs, rdna3amdgpu=0): BIG_PAGE WRONG: fragment-below-64KiB`: placement right, only the PTEs wrong. WHY the default path had 4 KiB PTEs (certain from git): it is the original Stage 2/4 walker (8d0e01e, one PTB entry per page); amdgpu's update path with fragments and huge PDEs came later (1ebd50f) behind rdna3amdgpu=1 as part of the whole amdgpu sequence, never for a problem with fragments; step `amdgpu` (card run 33) passed DMA, GFX, Vulkan and Metal with it. FIX (hw/gpuvm.c): the default path writes VRAM mappings (gpuvm_map_linear: GEM_VA of a VRAM BO, seq64, user-queue objects) in amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment steps with AMDGPU_PTE_FRAG in every PTE (`ptb_fragment`, `ptb_write`); it keeps its PTB-only tables, so a step of 2 MiB or more (a huge PDE in amdgpu) is written as PTEs of fragment 8 (1 MiB, the largest amdgpu_vm_ptes_update puts into a PTB), and a step is capped at the PA's alignment (amdgpu's drm_buddy blocks give that for free, our range allocator only the BO's alignment). The ring now gets 8 (1 MiB) x 4 + 5 (128 KiB): `PTE fragment 5 (128 KiB, amdgpu_vm_pte_fragment in 4 KiB PTEs, rdna3amdgpu=0): BIG_PAGE ok`. System pages keep 4 KiB PTEs (amdgpu fragments contiguous system runs too; left out on purpose, the IOMMU / bus addresses are not ours to promise). An unmap or a mapping over part of an old one re-fragments what is left of a fragment it cut (`ptb_refrag_edges`; amdgpu maps the rest of a split mapping again, amdgpu_vm_bo_clear_mappings -> amdgpu_vm_bo_update); our UAPI only ever unmaps whole mappings, so this is the safety net. TLB flushes unchanged; rdna3amdgpu=1 unchanged. Boot-arg `rdna3vmfrag=0` brings back the 4 KiB PTEs (`RDNA3,VMFragments` 0/1/2 = off / default fragments / rdna3amdgpu=1, one `VM: VRAM mappings ...` line at init). sim_navi32 maps the run's ring (VA 0x800000200000 / PA 0x1a200000) and checks every PTE's fragment against what the TLB may assume (`test_vm_frag_check`), PA-skewed and unaligned mappings, GTT, a hole in a 1 MiB fragment, a REPLACE over part of it, rdna3vmfrag=0. The triage row `attribute ring` reads the newest serial-*.log next to the report too (`report_serial`). NEXT RUN: `rdna3vm step ladder` again: the baseline must now be clean too and `attribute ring` PASS; the `attrring` rung stays in the ladder as the regression check (both clean); then drop it.
