| N58 | Boot hang, WindowServer never reaches the desktop (card run 2026-10-26j, macOS 26.4.1, `-cpu host`: every boot; Skylake-Client: rarely, card run 26e's AGPMEnabler busy timeout). ROOT CAUSE, certain from the boot watchdog's kernel stackshot (`bootwatch-20261026-135340-1/-2.spindump`, identical a minute apart, "Deadlocked: 2 threads - AppleGPUWrangler, thread call high #9"), frames resolved against the running binaries (the Tahoe accelerator build 5e33ae5597 of the bundle; IOAcceleratorFamily2 / IOGraphicsFamily / AppleGPUWrangler from `reference/macos-26.4.1/kc/SystemKernelExtensions.kc`, UUIDs equal to the spindump's): (a) thread call `rd3AccelBootGates` (tail call) -> `triggerFromSysctl(4)` (holds fRegLock) -> `registerFramebufferLocked` -> `deliverModeChange` (onCountGrewLocked, head 1 just got pipe 1) -> `IOAccelLegacyDisplayMachine::display_mode_did_change` (takes the ACCELERATOR LOCK, IOGraphicsAccelerator2 +0x88) -> per pipe `IOAccelLegacyDisplayPipe::displayModeDidChange` -> `IOFramebuffer::getAttributeExt` -> `IOGraphicsWorkLoop::timedCloseGate` on the CONTROLLER GATE - blocked by (b); (b) AppleGPUWrangler -> `IOFramebuffer::message` (inside the CONTROLLER GATE) -> `IOFramebuffer::probeAccelerator` -> `IOGraphicsAccelerator2::requestProbe(1)` -> IOLockLock(ACCELERATOR LOCK) - blocked by (a); (c) thread call high #10 `IOFramebuffer::powerStateWillChangeTo` -> the controller gate, and WindowServer's `IOFramebufferOpen` behind it ("PM waiting on pmDriverCallout ... RDNA3Framebuffer"). IOGraphics' own order is gate -> accelerator lock (its framebuffer notifications reach the display machine through `IOAccelDisplayPipe::display_change_handler` inside the gate; probeAccelerator too); our thread call took them the other way round. Timing decides whether AppleGPUWrangler's message to the newly published head 1 overlaps the registration, hence `-cpu host` vs Skylake. FIX (done): `RDNA3Accelerator::runInFbGates` closes the controller gate of every published head (`IOFramebuffer::getWorkLoop`, the IOGraphicsControllerWorkLoop; recursive for its owner; each workloop once, head order, `common/rdna3_fbgate.h`, `tests/test_fbgate.c` with a lock-order model) through nested `IOWorkLoop::runAction` before `registerFramebufferLocked` / `deliverModeChange` (sysctl `debug.rdna3accelfb` and the boot-arg thread call); lock order fRegLock -> controller gates -> accelerator lock. ECC review of 0535fd7: a head can publish while the thread waits for a gate (fbPublished sets fFBs[] without fRegLock), so inside the innermost gate the heads are planned again and the body runs only when every gate they need is held (else all gates opened and planned again, at most `RD3_FBGATE_TRIES` 4, then `NOTHING registered`); `registerPendingLocked` hands only heads of that plan to `found_framebuffer` (a later one: `published after its controller gate was planned ... NOT registered now`, left to its own boot-arg thread call, queued after fFBs[] is set, or the next sysctl write); the family's sweep (`debug.rdna3accelfb=2`) is refused while a published head is outside the gates. Log: `N58 <why>: inside <n> framebuffer controller gate(s) (heads 0x..), then the display machine (...)`, a head without a controller workloop named, a refused runAction named. Triage: `tools/triage_bootwatch.py` reads the bootwatch spindumps' `Deadlock:` verdicts (KERNEL DEADLOCK in the BOOTWATCH row, our kext named when in the cycle). OPEN (needs the card): the desktop on `-cpu host`; our own accelerator-lock holders (`rd3FamLock`) that call the framebuffer must not reach a controller gate (none known). VERIFIED on the card, run 2026-10-26 23:10 (CI run 55, 95f527c, -cpu host, 8 cores): the serial log has `N58 debug.rdna3accelfb>=3: inside 1 framebuffer controller gate` (both heads share one controller workloop), WindowServer opened the accelerator (VMID 2), no BOOTWATCH, baseline glyphs CLEAN 18/18, attribute ring PASS. The earlier -cpu host boots (20:01, 20:09, 21:04, 21:27, 21:51) all hung in this cycle. | accel, triage | S | fix done, needs card |
| N59 | Continuous animations stutter and step BACK to an earlier frame (card run 2026-10-26 app survey, reports/2026-10-26k: a CSS rotating box in Safari, transitions; frames shown 1,2,3,1,4,2,5,...). EVIDENCE (certain from the report and the code): all 0 legacy swaps / copy forwards; every frame reaches the panel through `RD3DisplayPipe::performTransaction`'s CPU COPY of the presented IOSurface's system memory (`iop: K6 transaction ... from the IOSurface's memory`, `debug.rdna3accel_iop_flip 0`); WindowServer's display surfaces are kept out of A3 (`a3display_refused 99`), so the Metal plugin draws them into a private image and puts the pixels into the IOSurface with the CPU only after the GPU finished (`-nvmtlSurfaceOut`, `census: ... 27780 copied out`); the plugin trace shows WindowServer composites each display (surfaces 1 / 10, load=Load) and then fills one buffer of a small present ring per frame (surfaces 24 / 34 / 36 in turn). Nothing orders the copy-out before the transaction's copy: a transaction that arrives first copies the frame the buffer held a ring ago = the owner's step back. Whether WindowServer's present path waits for completion (`commitAndWaitUntilSubmitted` waits, NullMoth's rule) or only for SCHEDULED (SkyLight / QuartzCore also use `waitUntilScheduled` / `addScheduledHandler:`, and the plugin marks a command buffer scheduled at its first submit, before any IOSurface publish) is NOT certain from the code: the card run decides. MEASUREMENT + FIX: (1) kernel, `common/rdna3_iop_stale.h` (`tests/test_iop_stale.c`): every IOP copy compares the buffer's IOSurface seeds (`IOSurface::getLockSeed / getUnlockSeed`, moved by the copy-out's write lock) with those at this head's last copy of it; unchanged = STALE (nothing wrote it since), then the copy waits, bounded, polling every 1 ms (`debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale_wait_ms`, boot-arg `rdna3iopstalewait`, default 12, 0 = count only; only the done write ends a wait (the unlock seed moved; a lock seed alone is a writer still copying); the wait is bounded by elapsed time; timed-out waits are capped at 48 ms per head per one-second window (`iop_stale_capped` counts the windows, logged), a wait that saw the write is not charged (ECC review of 5a8765b)) - the port of amdgpu's implicit sync of a scanout BO (amdgpu_dm_commit_planes -> dma_resv_wait_timeout); counters `debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale{,_checked,_fresh,_moved,_timeout,_nowait,_capped,_torn,_waitus,_wait_maxus}`, log `iop stale: ...` (rate-limited, first 8 then every 1024th). `iop_stale_moved` > 0 PROVES the race (the write came during the wait). (2) plugin, `metal/plugin/nvmtl_handoff.h` (`tests/test_handoff.c`): per command buffer that writes an IOSurface, whether its scheduled point was observed (a scheduled handler dispatched / waitUntilScheduled returned / -status read Scheduled; the gap is measured from the moment it was marked scheduled) before its last IOSurface write was published, `vk: handoff: SUMMARY cbs= early= by_handler= by_wait= by_status= gap_us_avg= gap_us_max= held= held_us_avg= hold=` and `handoff (N59): command buffer N wrote IOSurface S; its scheduled point was seen ... BEFORE ...`; A/B `NVMTL_SCHED_AFTER_PUBLISH=1` (rdna3mtl.conf, default off) holds the scheduled point until that publish (Metal allows a late scheduled; the fix for producers that hand a surface over at scheduled). (3) the visible proof: the app survey's Safari stage `frames` (frameorder.html: frame counters as strips, `common/rdna3_strip.h`: CSS steps() animation / canvas 2D / WebGL scissor clears) read from the scanout by `rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip FILE SECONDS` (one native row per sample, every change written) in two 10 s bursts: as booted, then with the kernel wait off (`frames-nowait`); `tools/frame_order.py` decodes (`41-ladder-frameorder-<burst>.log`). Triage rows `iop stale`, `handoff`, `frame order: N regressions in M samples (layer: css/canvas/webgl, pipe P)`. OPEN (needs the card): which side is late (kernel rows vs handoff rows); the regressions with the wait vs without it; a STALE timeout rate high enough to cost frames (legit re-presents); the plugin knob's A/B is a separate run (`NVMTL_SCHED_AFTER_PUBLISH=1` in rdna3mtl.conf, WindowServer restarted); the zero-copy IOP flip and the legacy swap paths do not check seeds yet (both off in this configuration). CARD RUN 2026-10-28 (app survey, plugin with the hand-off counters, kernel wait on): the strips stepped back with the kernel wait (18 regressions, css + canvas) and without it (8), jumps of up to 19 frames; the kernel saw 1133 STALE copies (583 got the write in the wait, 491 timed out) - the kernel wait is not the fix. The plugin hand-off showed the cause: WindowServer saw 12953 of 19533 IOSurface-writing command buffers scheduled before their copy-out was in the surface (scheduled handler 7425, waitUntilScheduled 5528; gap avg 20 ms, max 411 ms). NVMTL_SCHED_AFTER_PUBLISH is therefore ON by default now (=0 turns it off); the next app survey must show 0 frame-order regressions and `held` in the hand-off rows. Preview no longer crashes (N60: 15 of 15 apps ran). | accel, plugin, tools, triage | M | measurement + bounded wait done, needs card |
| N60 | Preview crashed opening a JPEG (card run 2026-10-26k, app survey, macOS 26.4.1): `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` at 0 in `CIKernelReflection::consolidate` <- `-[CIKernel _initWithString:andCIKernelLibrary:...]` <- `+[CIColorKernel kernelWithString:]` (ImageKit `_clampImageAlphaTo01`); the plugin's last line was `newLibraryWithSource: Apple's _MTLLibrary (7112 B container) -> ours`. CERTAIN FROM CODE (CoreImage 26.4.1 decompiled with REA/Ghidra): CoreImage turns the CIKL source into Metal source of `[[stitchable]]` functions (`CIKLLibraryMaker`, `-[CIKernelLibrary initWithSource:error:]` = `newLibraryWithSource`, language 4.0), then `CIKernelReflection::reflect(CIKernelLibrary*, name)` reads the library's MTLFunction: `functionType` (5 = stitchable), `returnType.dataType`, and `arguments` (each `type`, `dataTypeDescription.dataType`, struct `typeName` / `members`) into a vector of argument types; a function with no arguments is accepted. `consolidate()` copies the FOSL (source) reflection's argument types over that vector element by element with no size check: the library's vector was empty (`-[NVMTLFunction arguments]` answered nil for any visible function that is not an `!air.ci` function or a `air.stitching_info` stitch result), the source's was not, so the first store went through NULL. `+[...CIKernelLibrary refelectionWithFunctionName:]` is not involved (our library does not answer `reflectionForFunctionWithName:`, CoreImage reads the function). FIX (plugin, `nvmtl_ci_resolved`, NVMTLObjects.m; pure part `nvmtl_ci_refl.h`, `tests/test_ci_refl.c`): `arguments` / `returnType` of a visible function come from the first source that finds an argument: `!air.ci`, the stitching entry, and NEW a plain `!air.visible` entry (`ptr @f, !{outputs}, !{inputs}`, inputs by `air.arg_type_name` / `air.arg_name`, the 14 CoreImage struct names of `reflect()` as one argument each); the function-node match now accepts a quoted name (`ptr @"ns::f",`, LLVM quotes names with `::`), which the old bare match skipped. When none finds an argument the answer is ONE poison argument (binding type 18, data type None): `reflect()` refuses it (`CIKernelError` 3, `Unsupported type for parameter`), `_initWithString:` then skips `consolidate()` and builds the kernel from the FOSL reflection alone (the path of `CI_USE_MTL_DAG_FOR_CIKL_SRC=0`) instead of crashing; the plugin logs `CI REFL FAILED <fn>: no argument list could be read ...` and up to 36 `CI REFL FAILED diag <fn>: <AIR line>` lines (the `define`, the `!air.visible` / `!air.ci` lists and the nodes naming the function), so the AIR shape is in the report if it still comes to that. Success logs `CI PLAIN REFL <fn>: functionType 5, returns <dt>, <n> argument(s) ...` (`CI REFL` / `CI STITCH REFL` for the older entries). OPEN (needs the card, `full: true` CI + the owner rebuilds the plugin in the VM, `build_metal.sh`; app survey `preview-image` rung): (a) which of the three answers Preview's `_clampImageAlphaTo01` gets (the log line says); (b) with a CI REFL FAILED line, whether the FOSL-only kernel renders (JPEG shown, no crash) and what `diag` shows, then port that AIR shape; (c) with a PLAIN line, that the image renders and no `Unsupported type for parameter` follows. | metal/plugin, tests, docs | S | done (host test); card run open |
| N61 | The screen is slow although apps draw fast (card run 2026-10-28b, app survey after N59/N62): the frame-order page drew ~59 frames a second, the scanout showed 44 distinct frames in a 10 s burst (4.4 fps, `frame order` rows now say `displayed X fps of the page's Y fps`). EVIDENCE (certain from the report and the code): WindowServer's display surfaces are kept out of A3 (N27), so it composites into PRIVATE images (census: `surface-in uploads 0 into family VRAM + 4239 into private images`, `16330 copied out`); `-nvmtlSurfaceOut` put each finished frame into the IOSurface after the GPU with `nvmtl_vk_image_read`: an image -> staging buffer copy submitted ON ITS OWN and waited for, then a CPU memcpy from the staging buffer (memory type 2, HOST_VISIBLE|COHERENT system memory, not VRAM through the BAR) into the surface, on the one execution thread that advances every command buffer; the plugin trace's 781 kept 1920x1080 copy-outs: median 1.5 ms, average 10 ms, one in ten over 24 ms (a cluster at 23-25 ms, on both displays' buffers), longest 251 ms, and a 0.9 MB window surface re-uploaded every frame (surface-in 5 ms avg, up to 122 ms) - the variance says the separate submit + wait queues behind other GPU work (the split below decides). N59's hold kept each such command buffer unscheduled until that copy-out (`held 15858 (avg 12147 us)`), then the kernel CPU-copies the surface into the scanout (`performTransaction`, never timed before). DONE: (1) measurement: plugin `metal/plugin/nvmtl_present_cost.h` (`tests/test_present_cost.c`): every copy-out by path with bytes and time, the CPU path split into IOSurfaceLock / the staging submit + fence wait / the CPU copy (MB/s), slow ones (>= 16 ms), why the GPU path was not taken, every surface-in upload; `vk: present cost: SUMMARY outs= cpu= dma= out_us_avg= ... dma_mode=` (shared 10 s timer), `present cost (N61): copy-out #N ...` (first 8, every 1024th, the first 16 slow ones); kernel `common/rdna3_iop_copy.h` (`tests/test_iop_copy.c`): `debug.rdna3accel_iop_copy_{n,bytes,us,max_us,64}`, `iop_copy_{n,us}_h<i>`, `iop_gate_us{,_h<i>}` (the N59 stale wait + page-off wait), log `iop: copy #N (head H): ... bytes in U us (M MB/s, B-byte accesses)`; triage rows `present cost` (per process: `copy-out avg A ms (path ...), IOP copy avg B ms, surface-in avg C ms | ...`) and `IOP copy` (`tools/triage_present_cost.py`, `tests/test_triage_present_cost.py`). (2) the GPU copy-out (default ON, `NVMTL_SURFOUT_DMA=0` = the old path, also in the NVMTL_PARANOID profile and `vk: ab switches`): at a part's seal, before it can be submitted, each private surface image it wrote is copied into the IOSurface's own pages at the end of its command buffer (`nvmtl_surface_dma_record` -> `nvmtl_vk_cmd_surface_dma`: vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer at the surface's bytes per row and plane offset, then a transfer -> host barrier); the pages are imported once per texture (`nvmtl_so_import`, at the publish of its first copy-out, which is a CPU one, on the execution thread, so per-frame wrappers never import and the commit thread never wires pages) through `nvmtl_vk_buffer_import_host` (GEM_USERPTR, system memory the GPU maps snooped, `gpuvm_pte_flags`), only when `nvmtl_so_plan` accepts the geometry (single sample, no blocks, 1/2/4/8/16-byte elements, page-aligned base, plane offset and pitch whole elements, rows inside the allocation) and every page is writable in the process (`mach_vm_region`: a read-only page would be wired read-only and a GPU write there a VM fault). The publish (`-nvmtlSurfaceOutDMA`) only takes and gives back the IOSurface write lock so the seeds move (the kernel's N59 gate, surface-in, every other reader see the new frame); N59's hold then ends at GPU completion. A surface whose base moved since the import gets the CPU copy-out and no GPU copies again (`why_moved`); every other reason keeps the CPU path for that texture and is counted (`why_off/first/shape/ro/import/moved/record`). Frame trace path 7 `COPY-OUT(GPU into the surface, N61)`. ECC cpp-reviewer round 1 (077044e..a22df1a): a part with a GPU copy-out is never submitted ahead (G-SUB): its copy writes the surface without a lock or a seed move, so it runs at its turn, after every earlier command buffer is published (`commit: G-SUB N part(s) with a GPU copy into an IOSurface (N61) NOT submitted ahead`, first then every 65536th); the reason a copy was not recorded is carried by the part to its publish (`-nvmtlSurfaceOutWhy:`); the IOP copy refuses a source or scanout pitch that is not whole 32-bit words (the old loop stepped by words). (3) kernel: the IOP copy moves 8 bytes per access when every address and length is 8-aligned (same bytes, half the bus transactions). NOT DONE: the IOP copy by SDMA (A2's linear copies, `hw/xfer.c`): it needs the IOSurface's pages GART-mapped in the kernel per transaction and a wait for the SDMA fence before performTransaction returns (the surface may be reused right after) - left until the `IOP copy` row says the CPU copy matters; the per-frame re-upload of a small window surface (surface-in, a kernel import refused EPERM 217 times: surface 94) is a separate item. NEXT CARD RUN (app survey; CI `full: true`, `build_metal.sh` in the VM): `present cost: WindowServer[pid]: copy-out avg` ~0.1 ms with `path GPU` (or `GPU n / CPU m` with m ~ the number of surfaces: `first`), `handoff` `held avg` near the draw time (was 12 ms), `frame order` displayed fps up from 4.4 (and still 0 regressions), `IOP copy` avg and gates; A/B `NVMTL_SURFOUT_DMA=0` only if a picture is wrong. | N62 | A bigger BAR0 (card run 2026-10-28c, `RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=10`): macOS 26.4.1 booted with BAR0 1 GiB, which OVMF put at 0x900000000 (its 64-bit window; QEMU's `info pci` at 8 and 40 s: still there), but both of the core's maps failed (`DRM/amdgpu UAPI ready: VRAM 16368 MiB (0 MiB visible)`, `cannot map BAR0 (VRAM aperture)`): Stage 2 stopped, the framebuffer called the head `outside BAR0`, the accelerator's MM commit `NOT MAPPED`, WindowServer never reached the GPU (bootwatch). The console at phys 0x900000000 worked (EFI GOP). CAUSE (certain from Apple's IOPCIFamily-726.0.5 source, needs one card run to confirm on 26.4.1's binary): `IOPCIConfigurator::addHostBridge` clears `kIOPCIConfiguratorPFM64` when CPUID says VMM (QEMU sets the hypervisor bit), so `probeBaseAddressRegister` leaves a 64-bit BAR's `maxAddress` at `IOPCIRangeInit`'s 0xFFFFFFFF and `IOPCIRangeListAllocateSubRange` clamps every free range to it: in a VM a BAR above 4 GiB is never kept where the firmware put it, q35 has no free 1 GiB-aligned GiB below 4 GiB, so BAR0 gets no `assigned-addresses` entry and the IOPCIDevice no IODeviceMemory (`getDeviceMemoryWithRegister` NULL -> `mapDeviceMemoryWithRegister` NULL). Commit 0ae3df7 ("at 40 s macOS had them unmapped") was the same thing. Separately `MAX_BAR_SIZE` = 1 GiB: a bigger BAR's size is cut to its low 32 bits (16 GiB -> 0) on any machine: the "theoretical 1 GB maximum" of OpenCore's `ResizeAppleGpuBars` (Configuration.tex; ocvalidate calls values above 10 unsupported by macOS, above 8 unstable across sleep). NOT the cause: kernel VA (`IOMemoryDescriptorMapAlloc` maps into `KMEM_RANGE_ID_DATA`, which `kmem_range_startup_init` sizes to what is left of the kernel VA after the pointer ranges, tens of GiB on x86_64) or the cache mode. Apple's AMD driver maps windows of a BAR (AMDSupport `ATIController::mapFramebuffer(bar, offset, length)` -> `mapPCISubRange`: `getDeviceMemoryWithRegister`, `IODeviceMemory::withSubRange`, `map(kIOMapAnywhere VERIFIED on the card, run 2026-10-28 22:29 (CI run 72, 29bb021, RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=9, no 64-bit PCI window): `VRAM aperture: BAR0 512 MiB at phys 0x80000000, mapped 512 MiB ..., visible heap 400 MiB (15600 MiB beyond it)`, WindowServer on the accelerator. 1 GiB below 4 GiB is not expected to fit q35 (MMCONFIG at 0xB0000000); 1 GiB needs the -hypervisor experiment (open). | kIOMapWriteCombineCache)`; disassembled from 26.4.1's KC). DONE: (1) `common/rdna3_bar_window.h` (`tests/test_bar_window.c`) + `kext/src/RDNA3BarMap.h` `rd3MapBar0Window`, used by `RDNA3Accel::drmInit` and `RDNA3Bringup::mapVram`: a kernel window of BAR0 through `withSubRange` + `map` like Apple's, at most `rdna3vramwin=<MiB>` (default 1024 = the largest BAR macOS assigns, so all of it; at least 64), the whole BAR when smaller (256 MiB: the same single map as before), a failed map retried with half the window down to 256 MiB; the visible heap is the window (user mappings and the framebuffer's physical aperture keep the whole BAR through the descriptor). The map KPI returns no IOReturn, so a failure is logged by stage: no IODeviceMemory (with BAR0's config-space address, the resizable BAR capability's size, the CPUID VMM bit and the reason: `the firmware put it above 4 GiB and macOS in a VM keeps PCI BARs below 4 GiB` / `BAR0 is bigger than 1 GiB ... ResizeAppleGpuBars=10`), `withSubRange` failed, or every window size tried. Log `VRAM aperture: BAR0 X MiB at phys P, mapped Y MiB (kernel window, rdna3vramwin cap C MiB), visible heap Z MiB (W MiB beyond it)` or `VRAM aperture: NOT MAPPED: ...: <why>`, the bring-up's `BAR0 VRAM aperture X MiB at phys P, kernel window Y MiB` or `cannot map BAR0 (VRAM aperture): <why>`; IORegistry `RDNA3,VRAMBar0MB` / `VRAMWindowMB` / `VRAMVisibleHeapMB` (accelerator), `RDNA3,VRAMApertureMB` (now BAR0's size) / `VRAMMappedMB` (bring-up). (2) Triage row `VRAM aperture` (`tools/triage_bar.py`, `tests/test_triage_bar.py`; also from a serial log alone): INFO `BAR0 X MiB, mapped Y MiB, visible heap Z MiB`, WARN when bigger windows failed, FAIL when nothing was mapped (the reason) or the bring-up could not map, FAIL for the old kext's `0 MiB visible`. (3) `rdna3vm`: no 64-bit PCI window for a BAR0 up to 1 GiB (`mmio64_mb`, `tests/test_vm_present_ab.sh`), so OVMF puts it below 4 GiB; above 1 GiB the window stays (OVMF's GOP) and the host log says macOS takes no such BAR. (4) Docs: TESTING-VM "A bigger BAR0" (`RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=9` next, then 10), INSTALL-OPENCORE section 5 (bare metal: Resizable BAR on = 16 GiB BAR0 = no aperture in macOS: `ResizeAppleGpuBars = 10` or 8, or ReBAR off; the old advice `-1` was wrong), TESTING-HW, STAGE3. NEXT RUN: `RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=9`: host log `OVMF 64-bit PCI window: 0 MiB (BAR0 512 MiB)`, guest BAR0 below 4 GiB, row `VRAM aperture: BAR0 512 MiB, mapped 512 MiB, visible heap ~400 MiB`, VISIBLE VRAM with fewer refusals. | N63 | The IOP copy by SDMA (card run 2026-10-28d, app survey after N61: displayed 7.7 fps of the page's 59.2, `present cost` `IOP copy avg 20.43 ms`, copy-outs held avg 4.7 ms): every presented frame, per head, `RD3DisplayPipe::performTransaction` CPU-copied ~8 MB (1920x1080 BGRA) of the IOSurface's system memory into the scanout through the write-combined BAR on WindowServer's thread, ~40 ms a frame with two heads: the cap on smoothness. DONE (port of amdgpu's GTT BO path: `amdgpu_ttm_alloc_gart` -> `amdgpu_gart_bind`, `amdgpu_copy_buffer`, `amdgpu_gart_unbind`): (1) `hw/xfer.c` `rdna3_gart_bind_pages` (contiguous runs in one `gmc11_gart_map`, TT PTE flags system / snooped / MTYPE_UC, both hubs' TLBs flushed as N54 found SDMA needs), `rdna3_gart_unbind_pages`, `rdna3_copy_gart_rect_to_vram` (`sdma60_copy_rect_sync`: one span when the pitches match, one COPY_LINEAR per row otherwise; fence waited), reproduced in `tests/sim_navi32.c` (scattered pages with a contiguous run, two frames from one binding, a range rebound at the same offset with other pages read from the new ones, refusals map nothing). (2) `common/rdna3_iop_copy.h` (`tests/test_iop_copy.c`): the core's GART range table `rd3_ig_*` (first fit, 32 slots, a handle = slot + generation so a handle from before a reset or of an unbound range names nothing), `rd3_ig_rect_ok`, the page list from a descriptor's segments `rd3_iop_pages_add`, the fallback reasons, the read-back sample grid. (3) Core: `rdna3IopSdma` (`rdna3k_abi.h` `RD3KIopSdma`, ops BIND / COPY / UNBIND, statuses OK / NODEV / STALE / NOSPACE / INVALID / FAILED; `RDNA3Accel::platformIopSdma` under the DRM lock, `RDNA3Bringup::iopGart*`): an area of the GART nothing else uses (offset 4 MiB to 32 MiB under the top: above the IH rings, below the transfer window and the uf / uq / coherence slots), reset with every new GART table (hwInit: boot, GPU reset, wake; off in ipSuspend / stop) - log `iop sdma (N63): GART area for the IOP copy at offset 4 MiB, X MiB`, failures `iop sdma (N63): op N failed ...` (first 8, every 1024th). (4) Accelerator: the display pipe's source slots (4 per pipe, each keeps a surface's descriptor prepared and mapped) now also hold the surface's GART range: bound at the slot's first SDMA copy from the bus addresses of an `IODMACommand` (44-bit, mapped, as the core's `gttWire`; kept prepared while bound; a descriptor not starting on a page is left to the CPU), unbound in `iopDropSlot` BEFORE the descriptor is completed (slot reused, pipe freed); a STALE answer (new GART table) binds again once. Every frame `rd3AccelIopBySdma` asks the core to copy and returns after its fence: the transaction still ends with the frame in the scanout (N59's stale gate runs before it, as before). The first 256 SDMA copies, then every 16th, read 16 pixels of the scanout back through the BAR against the source. Any failure copies that frame with the CPU, counted by reason (`core`, `pages`, `bind`, `copy`, `verify`, `off`, `scanout`: SDMA writes a scanout only when its VRAM offset is the memory at its BAR address, BAR0's base learned from the core's own allocations - a framebuffer on the boot console without the core's head info reports offset 0 for a console that may lie elsewhere); three strikes (copy / verify / a failed bind) in a row switch the path off until the sysctl is written. Switch: boot-arg `rdna3iopsdma` / sysctl `debug.rdna3accel_iop_sdma`, DEFAULT 1 (the port reuses the GART + SDMA pattern the page-off and eviction run on the card since N54, a wrong picture is caught by the read-back and falls back by itself, a core without the function answers unsupported and keeps the CPU path). Counters `debug.rdna3accel_iop_copy_sdma_{state,n,us,core_us,max_us,bytes,binds,bind_us,unbinds,ranges,verified,fb,fb_<reason>}` (`iop_copy_n/_us` now count the CPU copies, `iop_copy_{n,us}_h<i>` both paths); logs `iop sdma (N63): ... done by SDMA0 ...` at start, `iop: sdma bind #N`, `iop: sdma copy #N (head H): ... in U us (core C us, M MB/s), 16 samples read back match` (first 4, every 1024th), `iop: sdma -> CPU (head H, <reason> #k): ...`, `iop sdma (N63): switched OFF after 3 failures in a row`, `iop: sdma unbind #N`. Triage `IOP copy` shows both paths, the SDMA state and the fallbacks (WARN: switched off, copy / verify failures, on but unused); `present cost` takes the IOP average over both paths with the path (`tools/triage_present_cost.py`, `tests/test_triage_present_cost.py`); collector greps `iop_sdma`. (path SDMA)`, SDMA avg ~1-2 ms (was 20 ms CPU), `fallbacks to the CPU 0` (or a few `core` before the GART is up), `frame order` displayed fps well above 7.7 (still 0 regressions), no `verify` / `copy` fallbacks. | N64 | What a WindowServer frame costs, framebuffer fetch made cheaper (card run 2026-10-29a, app survey after N63: displayed 11.3 fps of the page's 59.7; WindowServer census 340136 draws, 314275 of them framebuffer fetch, 6365 surface-in uploads / 4984 MB). FINDING (certain from the report): the low displayed rate is mostly the PROBE's. WindowServer's plugin trace (`plugin_trace.py --rates`, second 0 = 19:34:28 UTC) commits 122 command buffers a second (two per frame: 61 fps, `in_upload` 63/s) in seconds 518-529, 533-534, 542-543 and 571-577, and 20-36 a second (10-18 fps) in 535-570, exactly while the frame-order bursts ran (12:43:36 and 12:43:48 local = seconds 548 and 560, 10 s each) and the looks before them: `rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip` read the scanout back to back (4864 samples in 10 s, ~2 ms per ioctl), each call holding the display lock (`RDNA3Accel::drmScanout`, `fDispLock`, also taken by `displayFlip` / `displayCursor` / `displayScanAddr`) while it reads 1280 pixels through BAR0. WHAT A FBFETCH DRAW COST (certain from code): one render pass carries the colour attachment as its own input attachment (GENERAL, by-region self-dependency), one descriptor set per pass, and before EVERY reading draw a global `VkMemoryBarrier` COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT/WRITE -> FRAGMENT_SHADER/INPUT_ATTACHMENT_READ, BY_REGION (`nvmtl_fbf_bind`); no render-pass split and no copy. In RADV (Mesa d381c361 `radv_cmd_buffer.c` `radv_src_access_flush` / `radv_dst_access_flush` / `radv_stage_flush`) a barrier without an image has image_is_coherent false and has_CB_meta true: CB flush + CB metadata flush + PS partial flush + a whole-GL2 invalidate per draw. Measured on the host's RX 7800 XT (RADV 26.2.5, `tests/vk_fbfetch_probe.c` case 5: 400 overlapping window-tile fetch draws into BGRA8 1920x1080): 1.456 ms with the memory barrier, 1.335 ms with an image barrier, 0.323 ms with none (2.8 / 2.5 us a draw): ~1 ms of GPU a WindowServer frame, not the cap on smoothness. No cheaper ordering exists on this hardware/driver: RADV offers neither VK_EXT_ nor VK_ARM_rasterization_order_attachment_access (dev host vulkaninfo); VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read needs the same barrier; VK_EXT_attachment_feedback_loop_layout keeps DCC decompressed (`radv_layout_dcc_compressed`). DONE: (1) the barrier is a `VkImageMemoryBarrier` on each attachment the draw reads (GENERAL -> GENERAL, its level / slice; legal inside the pass, VUID-vkCmdPipelineBarrier-image-04073), so RADV skips the GL2 invalidate (`radv_image_is_l2_coherent`); `nvmtl_fbf_barrier`, decision `nvmtl_fbf_barrier_mode` (nvmtl_fbfetch_plan.h, test_fbfetch_plan); A/B `NVMTL_FBF_MEMBAR=1` restores the old barrier (in `nvmtl_ab_knobs`); `vk: fbfetch: SUMMARY ... bar_img= bar_mem= bar_noimg=`, log `got the OLD global barrier` for a draw whose attachment has no image; the probe's ordering cases (40 overlapping RAW draws, 60 interleaved RAW/WAR draws, restarts, sRGB/UNORM/F16/F32) pass with both barriers on the host. (2) `vk: ws frame: SUMMARY` (nvmtl_ws_frame.h, test_ws_frame): per command buffer that drew, encode (begin -> submit), GPU (its TOP/BOTTOM timestamps), fence wait, passes, draws, fbfetch draws and barriers by kind, surface-in uploads, frames per active second and the busiest second; triage row `ws frame` (triage_present_cost.py). (3) `rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip` sleeps after each round as long as it took (50 % duty, at most 8 ms; `# pacing:` line), triage `frame order` says `probe paced` / `probe UNPACED`. NEXT RUN must show: `frame order` displayed fps near the page's with `probe paced`; `ws frame` WindowServer busiest second ~122, encode / GPU per frame, image barriers only; glyphs (Terminal) and Chess still clean. OPEN: if the paced burst still drops WindowServer, shorten the kernel's lock hold (read only the strip's 432 columns: a `ncols` input to RD_NR_XNU_SCANOUT) or read the scanout without fDispLock; the surface-in uploads (63/s, avg 2.3 ms, synchronous on WindowServer's encoding thread) are the next CPU cost (the surf-in cache N51 already skips unchanged surfaces; the window surface whose kernel import is refused EPERM stays a copy); removing the fbfetch barrier entirely would need raster-order access RADV does not offer, or a fragment-shader-interlock (POPS) storage-image path in the translator (big, ~1 ms/frame to gain). | VERIFIED on the card, run 2026-10-29 13:44 (plugin with the image barriers, kernel N63): WindowServer composed 38442 frames in 609 active seconds = 63.1 frames/s, avg 4.04 ms a frame (encode 3.56, GPU 0.49), 17.5 framebuffer-fetch draws a frame all with image barriers (OLD memory 0); Terminal glyphs CLEAN 18/18; 15 of 15 apps ran. The VM's zero-copy BGRA flip is unchanged (tried first; with the switch off nothing of N68 allocates or flips). Test: a host test of the pure detector / demotion (`metal/plugin/nvmtl_surface_share.h` + tests/test_surface_share.c, step 4). RW write`) and markGuilty names the client and pid, but not the BO; added: the faulting client's next refused submission (CS on a guilty VM) or context query looks the fault VA up in its own mappings, and a hit in a shared read-only BO is reported to the accelerator, which turns sharing off (debug.rdna3accel_a3share=0) and logs `surfshare: VM fault at VA 0x... in a read-only shared import of surface S plane P (importer pid X, producer pid Y): sharing turned OFF (a3share_fault_off n)`, counter `debug.rdna3accel_a3share_fault_off`. DEVIATION: EPERM does NOT import again: the producer's texture draws into the OLD allocation (its BO), so a record for the surface's NEW allocation would show WindowServer a frame nobody updates; the surface keeps today's dirty-refused CPU copy-out until the application imports it again by making a new texture. (4) CONSUMER (WindowServer): metal/plugin/nvmtl_ws_share.h (eligibility: usage ShaderRead (+PixelFormatView) only and no write-combined CPU mode, kernel switch 1, not killed, surface not demoted, session not stopped; `nvmtl_wss_plan`; the R1 detector; demotion; counters) used by NVMTLDevice.m `nvmtlNewSurfaceTexture` (only for a surface whose producer put a generation on it): tag current + same generation = a view of the cached read-only backing (`nvmtl_ro_cache`, also in the N73 keep ring), a new generation = import again, tag stale or ineligible usage = the private path without asking the kernel; `nvmtl_surface_image_create` builds a read-only import as `nvmtl_vk_image_create_buffer_alias_ro` (SAMPLED | TRANSFER_SRC image over a TRANSFER_SRC buffer) or gives it back; any import error = private path, surface demoted, counted per errno. NVMTLObjects.m: a sample of the alias is `nvmtl_ro_sample` (tag current = no upload `sh_skip`; stale = no upload either, `sh_stale`, the next wrappers are private), a publish / replaceRegion / upload of it is refused and counted (`sh_wr`), a recorded GPU write is only detected (`nvmtl_surf_dirty`: the kernel's read-only page will fault it and turn sharing off); R1: `nvmtl_ro_commit` records the tag generation of every sampled alias at the composite's commit, `nvmtl_ro_complete` compares at completion (`vk: a3share: surface S hazard #n: tag G0 at commit, G1 at completion`, `sh_war`, the surface demoted for its life, 4 hazards = `vk: a3share: OFF for this session after 4 hazards`). Counters (WindowServer's `vk: ws frame: SUMMARY`): sh_imp (read-only imports, re-imports included), sh_skip, sh_stale, sh_reimp, sh_refused (+ sh_eperm / sh_enospc / sh_enoent / sh_enodev / sh_enxio / sh_eother), sh_war, sh_demoted, sh_wr, sh_inelig, sh_off; triage row `ws share` (tools/triage_present_cost.py: WARN when applications shared and sh_imp is 0, on sh_war, sh_off, sh_wr, refusals). Log wording: `item 7: surface S WxH -> read-only shared VRAM (allocation #G)`. Kill switches: NVMTL_WS_SHARE=0 (knob listed in `vk: ab switches`), NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_SHARE=1. Tests: tests/test_surface_share.c (gate + record decision table), tests/test_ws_share.c (eligibility, plan, sample, hazard, demotion, errno slots, generation value, SUMMARY text), tests/test_triage_present_cost.py. ECC REVIEW FOLLOW-UP (quick/a3share): a writable import where a read-only one was planned is given back (nvmtl_wss_import_verdict, `sh_rw`, surface demoted); R1 compares the tag's (seed, generation) so the producer's CPU copy-out fallback counts; the gate's inputs are published by one release store; the wrapper decision and the producer prefilter are pure and tested (nvmtl_wss_wrapper_action, nvmtl_ps_wants). SWITCH SCOPE: `debug.rdna3accel_a3share` is live for WindowServer (every wrapper re-checks it) but an APPLICATION decides at texture creation - only drawables created after a 0 -> 1 flip live in VRAM and share; for the A/B restart the app (or its window). COST TO WATCH: every WindowServer wrapper of a shared surface does two IOSurfaceCopyValue (tag, generation) plus one at each commit and completion per sampled alias (watch ws_main_thread / rec_us_avg in the `ws frame` row). KNOWN LIMITS (steps 3-4): the stale case is decided at wrapper creation (an alias cannot take an upload and a texture's image is not swapped in place); a tag that goes stale between the wrapper and its sample is only counted (`sh_stale`, one frame may show the producer's last GPU frame); R1 sees only a next frame that FINISHED between commit and completion (the tag moves at the producer's publish); a GPU write recorded against an alias cannot be taken back by the plugin. SECURITY REVIEW FOLLOW-UP (ecc:security-reviewer over 6b3c35d..b9b4c72: no CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM): (LOW-1, version skew) the read-only import is OPT-IN: the plugin calls `drm_xnu_surface_accept_ro(1)` (not with NVMTL_WS_SHARE=0), libdrm_xnu then sets RD_XNU_SURFACE_BO_IN_ACCEPT_RO in the IMPORT request's tail, the uapi passes RD_SURFACE_BO_ACCEPT_RO to ops.surface_bo, the core sends RD3K_SG_ACCEPT_RO and the accelerator looks for a share only then; without it (an older plugin still loaded in a WindowServer that was not restarted after an update, whose writable alias would fault and take the desktop down) the import is refused exactly as before (log `surfshare: WindowServer's import of surface S plane P does not accept a read-only one`), and the uapi refuses an OS answer of gpu_ro to a caller that did not accept it (-EPERM, its hold given back); tests: tests/test_uapi.c (zero tail, 24-byte struct, forced gpu_ro: -EPERM; IN_ACCEPT_RO: SHARED_RO), test_libdrm_amdgpu. (LOW-2) R1 hazards are counted per PRODUCER: the import tail (out_ver 2) carries the producer's pid (`share_pid`, drm_xnu_surface_last_import_pid, nvk_buffer share_pid), 4 hazards of one producer demote that producer (`vk: a3share: producer pid P demoted after 4 hazards`, once; `sh_pdem`; none of its surfaces is imported read-only, the others keep theirs), the session stop is only a backstop at 64 hazards of all producers; hazard lines are rate limited per producer (its first 4, then every 64th); tests in tests/test_ws_share.c. OPEN from the review: (LOW-3) WindowServer's read-only imports are charged to its own per-file foreign-bytes cap RD_SURFACE_BO_FILE_MAX (half the visible VRAM): many shared drawables give it -ENOSPC (R4, the private path); a separate budget for read-only imports or charging the producer is open; (note) op 4 (the share record) takes the accelerator's family lock like DIRTY, once per (surface, generation) - watch the lock traffic on the card; (note) alloc_gen and share_pid are exposed to the importer in the tail (a generation counter and another process's pid, both of a surface the caller may already look up). A demoted surface ring of 128 can still be churned by an application that puts bogus `NVMTLShareGen` values on many surfaces (each failing import demotes its surface, an evicted genuine demotion gets one more chance; a demoted producer stays demoted whatever the ring holds). STILL OPEN: step 5 (CI `full: true` build of the plugin, the card round); R1's fence-based fix only if the card shows hazards; the fault attribution runs only at the faulting client's next refused submission or context query (a WindowServer that never submits again after its VM was marked guilty is not attributed: the VA is still in the core's `[gfxhub] page fault` line). | accel/uapi, metal/plugin | M | open: (a) done, (c) steps 1-4 done (3-4 not built on macOS yet), step 5 (CI + card) open |

Logs once per kernel through the new translator notes (src/notes.rs, wrapper export `nvmtl_translate_notes`, logged by NVMTLLibraryLoad.m after each translate and kept in release syslog by the `translator note: ` keyword): `translator note: <function>: metal2vulkan: kernel <fn>: pointer phi <name> merges Function and Private storage; demoted to a per-storage value select (DRIVER-PLAN N72)`; the wrapper's threadgroup-length, formatless-fallback and dump-failed messages go the same way. A copy of a split pointer (bitcast, freeze, inlined parameter) stays split; a phi or select taking a split pointer is refused by name (`... takes the split mixed-storage pointer <p> ...; nested mixed phis are not supported (N72)`, or `mixed-storage pointer phi <p> ... feeds another pointer phi / pointer select <s> ...`); any other shape still fails with the old message. FINDING (certain from code): a drawable's cycle is app encode -> commit -> the plugin's present call, made only after the GPU finished the presenting command buffer and its copy-out (NVMTLEventSync.m `nvmtlFinishExecution`; Apple presents at the scheduled point and orders WindowServer's reads in its kernel, ours cannot: WindowServer reads the IOSurface's pages with the CPU) -> WindowServer: a NEW texture per frame for the client's IOSurface (`newTextureWithDescriptor:iosurface:`, round 2 finding 10), whose image the plugin's cache held only weakly, so every frame made a new 8 MB private image (vkCreateImage, its memory, a view) and freed the one before, then a CPU upload of the frame (`nvmtlSurfaceIn`: memcpy into staging + a synchronous submit and fence wait on WindowServer's thread, behind whatever is queued on the GPU), the composite, `commitAndWaitUntilSubmitted` (waitUntilCompleted in WindowServer), the IOP transaction; the frame before is released only then. With three drawables and WindowServer slower than the app, a frame period is about (encode + commit->present + WindowServer's latency) / 2, so both halves cap it. DONE (`metal/plugin/nvmtl_drawable_life.h`, tests/test_drawable_life.c): (1) the keep ring: WindowServer keeps the last `NVMTL_SURFTEX_KEEP` (default 8 in WindowServer, 0 elsewhere, 0 in NVMTL_PARANOID) IOSurface images made or reused alive (bounded by a COUNT of images of any size, 8 x 4K BGRA ~ 265 MB; one unused for 2 s goes at the next texture made, else by the 10 s timer, up to ~12 s; evictions are released after the device lock on WindowServer's thread, the timer only when idle), so the next wrapper of a surface is an alias of its image (the path two live wrappers always took) and only the upload is left; (2) counters `vk: drawable: SUMMARY` per process (the nextDrawable wait timed by a hook of `-[CAMetalLayer nextDrawable]` in apps, `NVMTL_DRAWABLE_TRACE=0` off; commit -> present; present -> the same surface back from nextDrawable; IOSurface textures made / reused / kept / let go and a wrapper's life), triage row `drawable` (tools/triage_present_cost.py, WARN when an app waits over 30 %). CAUSE (the card's A/B): UCLK p-state switching - `rdna3_pmctl levels mclk 8` (UCLK pinned at 1218 MHz) no flash, `levels mclk 15` (96 MHz idle) the flash at once. With `rdna3mall` 0 (the default) the driver made no DC p-state decision; the PMFW switched UCLK when the arbiter allowed, the switch fitting the main head's vblank, not the secondary's (its first active lines then: underflow). FIX (port, `hw/dcn32_pstate.c` `dcn32_uclk_pin_decide` / `_init` / `_update` / `_power_lost`, `kext/src/RDNA3DisplayPower.cpp` `displayUclkPinInit` / `displayUclkPinUpdate` / `displayUclkPinSysctl`): what amdgpu does for two unsynchronised heads - `dml32_CalculateWatermarksMALLUseAndDRAMSpeedChangeSupport` (DRAMClockChangeSupportNumber: a second unsynchronised plane without active margin -> unsupported), no SubVP / FPO (`dcn32_full_validate_bw_helper`, `dcn32_can_support_mclk_switch_using_fw_based_vblank_stretch`) -> `p_state_change_support` false -> `dcn32_update_clocks` "set UCLK min = max" (`dcn32_smu_set_hard_min_by_freq(PPCLK_UCLK, top level)` over the DAL mailbox, with the hard-min status wait); switching supported again (one head) -> the hard minimum back to the lowest level (`dcn32_initialize_min_clocks`' DRAM clock); the levels from `dcn32_init_clocks` / `dcn32_get_memclk_states_from_smu` (read-only DAL messages). This path programs none of DC's DETs or watermarks, so no plane is credited with an active margin: two or more lit heads (not blanked by a power request, the `rdna3mirror=1` pipe counted) pin UCLK; one head keeps what ran before (nothing sent at all unless it was pinned). Decided at the bring-up (`RDNA3Bringup.cpp`: after the DMCUB's init and its `SetAlwaysWaitDmcubResp`, as amdgpu's dc_hardware_init follows dm_dmub_hw_init, and before the step-9b clone, whose raise then pins before the second head fetches), each mode set, mirror on / off, display sleep / wake (`rdna3dpms`), system wake (a power loss: the PMFW's own minimum again), the sysctl; a bring-up again after a pin sends its first decision whatever it is. Off: boot-arg `rdna3uclkpin=0`, live `sudo sysctl -w debug.rdna3_uclk_pin=0` (releases at once; 1 decides again). Triage row `UCLK pin (N74)` (`tools/triage_uclk_pin.py`, `tests/test_triage_uclk_pin.py`): FAIL a refused minimum, WARN two heads switching / unavailable / two heads without the property. Sim (`tests/sim_display_pstate.inc` `test_uclk_pin`): two 1080p60 heads with one DET segment each and the arbiter allowing -> the underflow model sees both at risk; the update sends SetHardMinByFreq(UCLK, 1218) and the risk is 0; one head -> 96 MHz; refused messages, a power loss and a re-init. OPEN: (a) amdgpu's own answer for 2x1080p60 is DC's DET split (`rdna3mall=2`: 9 + 9 segments, the sim's DML says vactive at state 0, so UCLK may drop to 96 MHz without a flash) - try `rdna3mall=2` on the Mac once this pin is confirmed; (b) DC's timing synchronisation of identical timings (`enable_timing_synchronization`) would give one shared vblank - not ported; (c) heads stay lit through macOS display sleep without `rdna3dpms`, so UCLK stays pinned then; (d) the hard-min status wait (`ReturnHardMinStatus`, up to 2 s under the display lock) has not run for UCLK on the card. | N75 | Geekbench 7 GPU on bare metal, round 3 (2026-11-01, build 02b2210): N72 never fired (no `translator note` line), `depthwiseConv_3x3` failed byte for byte as in round 2 (`Private pointer phi %metal2vulkan.ct.slot.48 has differing structural sources; raw construction failed: ... pointer merge storage mismatch Function vs Private at Local("%1425") across [Local("%72"), Local("%1425")]`, then `function constants: re-translate FAILED`) after ~280 s. FINDING (certain from code, reproduced with hand-made fixtures, no Apple IR): `%metal2vulkan.ct.slot.N` is the construct tree's dispatch-loop slot (src/native/cfg/structured_emit/construct_tree/renest.rs, whole-CFG dispatch; `ct.sl.slot.N` is the straddle one), not the function-constant path (that line is only the plugin's re-translate after specialization): `slot.N = phi [undef, pre], [nextslot.N, continue]`, `nextslot.N` merges each case's Function or Private value with `slot.N` itself. N72 split a phi only from incoming values already emitted, and a back-edge value never is: the slot (and, in raw construction, the original loop phi whose back-edge value `%1425` is a Function/Private phi emitted after it) fell through to the old paths. DONE (metal/translator): `src/native/emitter/pointers/mixed_network.rs` works out, per function before emission, the storages every address-space-0 pointer can come from (alloca Function, address-space-0 global Private, GEPs/copies their base's, phis the union, anything else unknown; worklist, linear); a value with exactly {Function, Private} is a network member, and all members are split the same way under names fixed in advance (arm `<v>.metal2vulkan.mixarm<g>`, a copy shares its source's; selector `<phi>.metal2vulkan.mixarm.cond`, GEPs/copies use their base's), with the arm phis and arm GEPs registered as forward TIR facts so the index-domain machinery resolves a forward arm. `emit_mixed_storage_pointer_phi` (phi_mixed_storage.rs) takes a forward member (its arms and selector), a forward value of one storage (its arm as it is; the other arm's zero from the nearest value of that storage in the network), undef (undef on both arms); members are split before the selected-access-tree path can take them (phi_compare.rs). A walk over a split pointer is now split too (N72's refusal of it is gone); a select of one, or a phi that also merges an unknown pointer, is still refused by name; a member that cannot be split after a phi took its arms is refused (`... cannot be split, but a phi already took <arm>; this network is not supported (N75)`), an arm that cannot take its zero path likewise (`... arm's index domain cannot take the arm's structural zero path (N75)`, `... arm has no value to take a structural zero path from (N75)`), never a module with an undefined id. ECC review of 14081f6 (rust-reviewer, HIGH-1: a network with two roots of one storage translated reading the first root only): the analysis also tracks each value's roots (allocas, Private globals; capped at 3), and a member phi whose inflow has more than one root of a storage is refused (`mixed-storage pointer phi <p>: its network has more than one <Function|Private> root (<a>, <b>) (N75)`); a phi taking a member that was emitted without its split is refused (`... takes member <m>, which was emitted without its split; this network is not supported (N75)`); a phi or select outside the network taking a copy of a member is refused like the member itself. Also fixed by it: a loop phi with a Function entry value and a forward Private-only phi on the back edge translated and validated but addressed the alloca on every iteration (the Private table was never read). Note line, once per kernel: `translator note: <function>: metal2vulkan: kernel <fn>: pointer phi <phi> merges Function and Private storage; demoted to a per-storage value select; its network is loop-carried (DRIVER-PLAN N72, N75)` (the old ending ` (DRIVER-PLAN N72)` when every edge was already emitted). Tests: metal/translator/translator/tests/mixed_storage_loop_phi.rs (17 fixtures after the review: the per-arm loads are pinned to the Function variable and the named Private table, two-root networks in loops and construct-tree cases, the same-storage second root, a store through a loop-carried split pointer, a phi merging a member with a loaded pointer, Function-only and Private-only loops; the first 8, spirv-val, all 8 fail at 1f6f8a7: Function/Private back edges, a loop carrying a mixed or a Private-only merge, the construct tree's slot as renest writes it with a case stepping the slot's own pointer, a loop exiting into its guard's join end to end through the straddle dispatch, the named refusal); mixed_storage_pointer_phi.rs's walk fixture now asserts the split. NEXT MAC RUN: Geekbench 7 GPU: `translator note: depthwiseConv_3x3: ... pointer phi %metal2vulkan.ct.slot.48 ... loop-carried (DRIVER-PLAN N72, N75)` (the phi name may differ) and no `link FAILED: "depthwiseConv_3x3"`; a failure now names N75 or `mixed-storage pointer phi <phi>: <storage> arm failed: <reason>`; the link time (`link cache: miss ... ms`). Left out on purpose (project decisions): HDCP / secure display TAs,
eDP / PSR / ABM, USB-C / DPIA, KFD / ROCm, RAS / ECC, XGMI.

## How much can be written without the card

- **Simulator-verifiable ports** (register sequences from named amdgpu
  functions: P1, P4, S1-S3, S5, S6, A2, V1, R1, R2, R5) can be written in
  large batches - several thousand lines before a card run - because the
  simulator checks the order and values against amdgpu. What stays unknown is
  how the card reacts; each batch therefore ships behind a boot-arg and the
  round's single big step turns all of them on, with variant fallbacks in code
  (as the accelerator's head strategies did).
- **Behaviour-dependent work** (P2, S2's exact clock path, A3, M2-M6, V3)
  depends on what macOS / the card do and cannot be written far ahead: one
  round per discovery. Here the gain is in making each round report more
  (pictures, power trend, mtlprobe, CTS) so no round is spent on a question
  the report could have answered.

## Test rounds

One boot per round (`rdna3vm queue pending`, step `big`), unattended, then
`rdna3vm result`. The report already holds: DMA/GFX tests, Vulkan, PM
trend, accelerator swap/flip log, and (from `944d983`) a picture of what
each display pipe scans out. Planned additions, each cheap: `mtlprobe` (M1),
CTS subset (V2), hang test (R1), soak (R2), mode switching with a picture
per mode (S4).

DSC over MST needs an MST hub with a DSC monitor behind it on the card: a
bench with one runs step `mst` with `rdna3dsc=1` and a mode beyond the

## Order

1. A1 (pictures confirm two heads) -> defaults on (A1, P3).
2. P1 + P2 diagnostics (idle power), S1 + S5 + S6, V1, R1 - one batch, one boot.
3. M1 (does Metal see the card at all?) - early, because it sizes finish line M.
4. S2-S4 (mode setting), A2.
5. P4/P5 (sleep), R2, R3 (bare metal).
6. M2 -> M6 iteratively; V2/V3 alongside.
7. R4, R5; R6 only if everything else holds.

## D4: display state across resets and sleep (2026-10-10, sim/host verified, step `sleep`)

What exists (`hw/dcn32_state.c`, `kext/src/RDNA3Resume.cpp`):

| Piece | amdgpu model | Behind |
|---|---|---|
| Snapshot of every lit head: the clone's per-instance register lists, DIG back end TMDS setup, routing, DCCG, DET, cursor, gamma mode, VBIOS ids, pixel clock; global DCHUBBUB system context / arbiter / DCCG part | `dm_suspend`'s cached state | always (reads only) |
| Check: power, OTG, blank, DIG, timing, system aperture, routing, registers that differ, frames | - | always (reads only) |
| Restore of a head (also a power-gated pipe with nothing left) in the `dcn32_modeset_apply` / `dcn32_clone_enable` order | `dm_resume` -> `dc_commit_streams` (`dcn20_enable_stream_timing`, `link_set_dpms_on`, `dcn32_hubp_pg_control`, `dcn32_program_det_size`, `dcn10_link_encoder_hw_init`) | `rdna3resume=1` |
| Monitor standby on any TMDS head (the boot heads too) | `link_set_dpms_off` / `link_set_dpms_on` (`dcn10_link_encoder_disable_output`, `dcn10_link_encoder_enable_tmds_output`) | `rdna3dpms=2` |
| After a GFX / SDMA soft reset: the heads checked (those resets never touch DCN) | - | always |
| After the mode-1 reset: the heads restored | `amdgpu_device_gpu_recover` -> `dm_resume` | `rdna3resume=1` |
| System sleep: snapshot, streams down | `amdgpu_dm_commit_zero_streams` | `rdna3resume=1` (snapshot always) |
| Wake: power loss detected (PCI config, `RCC_CONFIG_MEMSIZE`, BIOS scratch 7 ASIC_INIT_COMPLETE, PSP sOS, heads), published as `RDNA3,Wake*` | `amdgpu_device_need_post` | always |
| Wake after a power loss: `resetIfPspStuck` (a VM wake comes with a bus reset), ASIC_Init, the whole `hwInit()` again (PSP, firmware, SMU/PM, DMCUB, GMC, IH, SDMA, GFX, MES), then the heads restored | `amdgpu_device_resume` -> `amdgpu_device_asic_init`, `amdgpu_device_ip_resume` | `rdna3resume=1` |

The S3 gap - what a full resume still lacks, in the order it will bite:

1. **Suspend side.** Done (`a3b2ec6`, `rdna3resume=1`): `systemSuspend`
   runs `amdgpu_fence_driver_hw_fini` and `amdgpu_device_ip_suspend_phase2`
   (VCN/JPEG, MES, SDMA, GFX, PSP, IH, GMC, SoC); every wake then runs the
   resume. `smu_suspend` has nothing to send on 13.0.7 (mp1_state NONE).
2. **VRAM contents.** amdgpu evicts every VRAM BO to system memory before
   the sleep (`amdgpu_device_evict_resources`) and moves them back; here
   VRAM is simply lost: user BOs, the accelerator's and the framebuffer's
   surfaces (black until WindowServer redraws), and the **GPUVM page-table
   pool** - live VMs get their MMHUB/GFXHUB contexts back from `fVmPd` but
   the tables behind them are garbage, so old contexts fault (clients see
   VRAM lost). Page tables: done - after a VRAM loss the pool starts over,
   each live VM gets a new root and the UAPI maps every client's ranges
   again (`rdna3_drm_device_vm_replay`). BO contents: done - every DRM BO
   in VRAM, the seq64 slots, the accelerator's surfaces and the boot
   scanout are copied to wired system pages through SDMA before the
   suspend and back after a power loss (`kext/src/RDNA3Evict.cpp`).
3. **Display init.** `dcn32_init_hw` is not ported: the clock manager's
   raise half runs in the restore now (DISPCLK / DPPCLK hard minimums
   through the DAL mailbox, back to the saved clocks; `restored (-16)` only
   when that fails or DISPCLK sits at divider 127); DCFCLK and the rest of
   `dcn32_init_clocks` are not ported; DIO / PHY init of the unused
   links; the replay of the saved DCHUBBUB / DCCG globals stands in for
   `hubbub32_init`, `dccg32_init` and `hubbub3_init_dchub_sys_ctx`.
4. **Lost display contents.** The gamma / degamma LUT RAMs (a restore after
   a power loss sets them to bypass; the table macOS last sent is loaded
   again now, `displayGammaReload`), the VPG infoframe contents (infoframes off, as after a mode set),
   HDMI audio.
5. **DMCUB.** `rdna3dmub=3` attaches to the GOP's firmware; after a power
   loss or a mode-1 reset that firmware is gone and the re-init now loads
   our own, as `rdna3dmub=1` does (`RDNA3,DMUBReloaded`).
6. **DP heads.** Done for DP SST on one pipe with `rdna3dplink=1`: the
   sleep takes the link down (`link_set_dpms_off`), the restore brings
   the stream resources back (the link cap verified for a new sink,
   DTBCLK again for 128b/132b) and trains the link again
   (`link_set_dpms_on`); monitor standby goes through the link too. DP MST
   and ODM combined heads are still refused (blank only).
7. **Ordering with macOS.** The wake work runs on a thread call after
   `setPowerState(1)`; framebuffer power requests that arrive earlier are
   recorded and applied afterwards. A picture lost in VRAM (a wake or a
   mode-1 reset whose VRAM did not all come back) moves
   `RD3KDisplayHotplug.lost`; every framebuffer then raises its
   connect-change interrupt, so IOGraphics probes the head again and
   WindowServer draws everything again (`RDNA3,DisplayPictureLost`,
   `RDNA3,PictureLost` on the framebuffer).
8. **Hotplug across the sleep.** Done: the HPD looks stop before the sleep
   (`amdgpu_dm_irq_suspend`; after a power loss the pins read unplugged
   until they are enabled again), the wake work programs the HPD pins and
   filters again (`link_resume`) and the looks start after the longest
   connect filter; a monitor plugged or pulled while asleep counts as a
   hotplug then.
